The global dynamics for the Maxwell-Dirac system Thanks: 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification. 35Q41, 35Q55, 35Q40. Thanks: Keywords and phrases. Maxwell-Dirac system, Lorenz gauge, global existence, modified scattering, space-time resonance, vector-field energy method.
Yonggeun Cho, Kiyeon Lee

Abstract
. In this paper, we study the global existence and modified scattering of solutions to (1+3) dimensional massive Maxwell-Dirac system in the Lorenz gauge. We employ a vector-field energy method combined with a delicate analysis of the space-time resonance argument. This approach allows us to establish decay estimates and energy bounds crucial for proving the main theorem. Especially, we provide an explicit phase correction arising from the strong nonlinear resonances.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Maxwell-Dirac system

In this paper we consider (1+3)-dimensional Maxwell-Dirac system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} i{\alpha}^{\mu}\textbf{D}_{\mu}\psi&=m\beta\psi,\\
\partial^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}&=-\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle,\end{aligned}\right.

(MD)

where the spinor field is
\psi:{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{4}
, the gauge fields are
A_{\mu}:{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, the covariant derivative
\textbf{D}_{\mu}
denotes
\partial_{\mu}-iA_{\mu}
for
\mu=0,1,2,3
, and
\partial_{0}=\partial_{t}
. The curvature is defined by
F_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}
.
{\alpha}^{\mu}
and
\beta
are Hermitian
4\times 4
matrices. In particular,
{\alpha}^{0}
denotes the
4\times 4
identity
I_{4}
and
{\alpha}^{1},{\alpha}^{2},{\alpha}^{3},\beta
have the relation

\displaystyle{\alpha}^{j}{\alpha}^{k}+{\alpha}^{k}{\alpha}^{j}=2\delta^{jk}I_{4},\;\;{\alpha}^{j}\beta+\beta{\alpha}^{j}=0\;\;(j,k=1,2,3),\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\beta^{2}=I_{4}.

We use the standard Pauli-Dirac representation
[
2
,
44
]
in this paper. The Greek indices indicate the space-time components
\mu,\nu=0,\,1,\,2,\,3
and Roman indices mean the spatial components
j=1,\,2,\,3,
in the sequel. The Einstein summation convention is in effect with Greek indices summed over
\mu=0,\cdots,3
and Roman indices summed over the spatial variables
j=1,2,3
. We lower and raise indices with Minkowski metric
\eta={\rm diag}(-1,1,1,1)
. Thus
{\alpha}_{\mu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}\alpha^{\nu}
and
\partial^{\mu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}
. The
\langle\phi,\psi\rangle=\phi^{\dagger}\psi
denotes a standard complex inner product. We call
massive
and
massless
(
MD
) if the mass parameter is
m>0
and
m=0
, respectively.

Maxwell-Dirac system is the Euler-Lagrange equations for
\textbf{S}[A_{\mu},\psi]
, where

\displaystyle\textbf{S}[A_{\mu},\psi]=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}}\left[-\frac{1}{4}F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}+i\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}^{\mu}\textbf{D}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle-m\left\langle\psi,\beta\psi\right\rangle\right]dxdt.

The system (
MD
) models an electron in electromagnetic field and form a fundamental system in quantum electrodynamics. For detailed description, we refer to
[
2
,
42
]
.

One of the basic features of (
MD
) is the gauge invariance. Indeed, (
MD
) is invariant under the gauge transformation
(\psi,A)\longmapsto(e^{i\chi}\psi,A-d\chi),
for a real-valued function
\chi
on
{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. For the sake of concreteness of discussion, let us choose the Lorenz gauge

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu}=0.

(1.1)

Under (
1.1
), the system (
MD
) becomes

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} (-i\partial_{t}+\alpha\cdot D+m{\beta})\psi&=A_{\mu}\alpha^{\mu}\psi\quad\mathrm{in}\;\;\mathbb{R}^{1+3},\\
-\square A_{\mu}&=\left\langle\psi,\alpha_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle,\end{aligned}\right.

(1.2)

The momentum operator
D=(D_{1},D_{2},D_{3})
is defined by
D_{j}=-i\partial_{j}\;(j=1,2,3)
and
{\alpha}=({\alpha}^{1},{\alpha}^{2},{\alpha}^{3})
and
\square=-\partial_{t}^{2}+\Delta
. We will consider a Cauchy problem of (
1.2
) with initial data

\displaystyle\psi(0)=\psi_{0},\quad A_{\mu}(0)

\displaystyle=a_{\mu},\quad\partial_{t}A_{\mu}(0)=\dot{a}_{\mu}.

(1.3)

If the solution to
(
1.2
)
with (
1.3
) is sufficiently smooth, the system possesses the charge conservation law

\displaystyle\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}=\|\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}.

1.2.
Previous works

There is a large amount of literature dealing with the local and global well-posedness, and the asymptotic behavior of solutions of IVP to (
MD
). For early work in
[
21
,
3
]
, the authors considered the local well-posedness of (
MD
) on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}
and Georgiev
[
17
]
proved the global existence for small, smooth initial data in the Lorenz gauge. Later, D’Ancona-Foschi-Selberg
[
8
]
obtained an almost optimal regularity
(\psi_{0},a_{\mu},\dot{a}_{\mu})\in H^{\varepsilon}\times H^{{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}}\times H^{{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{2}}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}
of local solution in the Lorenz gauge. They exploited the spinorial null structures, which stem from Dirac projection operators. D’Ancona-Selberg
[
9
]
extended their previous approach to (
MD
) on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2}
and proved the global well-posedness in the charge class
L^{2}\times H^{\frac{1}{2}}\times H^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Regarding the local well-posedness for (
MD
) in the Coulomb gauge (
\partial^{j}A_{j}=0
), we refer to
[
1
,
34
]
. Moreover, Masmoudi and Nakanishi
[
35
]
showed the unconditional uniqueness results for (1+3) dimensional (
MD
) in the Coulomb gauge.

Concerning the asymptotic behavior of global solution to (
MD
) in the
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}
Minkowski space, we refer to
[
14
,
39
]
. In
[
14
]
, Flato et al considered a final state problem of (
MD
) in the Lorenz gauge and they showed the asymptotic behavior and asymptotic completeness which leads to the global well-posedness of IVP to
massive
(
MD
) for a data set in Schwartz class and also to a modified scattering. The result of
[
14
]
seems to be the first nonlinear scattering result obtained without compact support condition. Later, Psarelli
[
39
]
showed the global behavior for the IVP to
massive
(
MD
) in the Lorenz gauge with compactly supported initial data.
Regarding (
MD
) with vanishing magnetic field, in
[
6
,
7
]
, the authors established the modified scattering results in the Lorenz gauge, independently. Recently, Herr, Ifrim, and Spitz
[
26
]
considered (
MD
) in the Lorenz gauge and showed the global existence and modified scattering based on the method of testing with wave packets. Especially, they investigated the asymptotic behavior of the solutions via asymptotic expansion inside the light cone.

For other problems related to (
MD
), we first refer to
[
16
]
for a modified scattering in the energy critical space on
\mathbb{R}^{1+d}\;(d\geq 4)
in the Coulomb gauge and also
[
33
]
for a linear scattering on
\mathbb{R}^{1+4}
in the Lorenz gauge. As a scalar counterpart of (
MD
), the Maxwell-Klein-Gordon system (MKG) has been also studied by many authors. Regarding the global well-posedness for the (1+4) dimensional
massless
(MKG), we refer the reader to
[
32
,
41
]
. In
[
31
,
36
,
37
]
, the authors studied independently the scattering results for
massless
(MKG) in the Coulomb gauge on
\mathbb{R}^{1+4}
. Concerning the global existence for (1+3) dimension, we refer to
[
43
]
. In particular, in
[
45
,
46
,
5
,
25
]
, the authors presented an asymptotic behavior of the solution to the
massless
(MKG) in the Coulomb and Lorenz gauge on
\mathbb{R}^{1+3}
. We also refer to
[
28
,
38
]
for the modified scattering results of the
massive
Maxwell-Klein-Gordon type system in the Lorenz gauge. While the
massless
(MKG) are concerned by many authors, there is a few scattering results for
massive
(MKG). We would like to mention the recent result in
[
15
]
which establishes the global regularity for the modified scattering results of the higher dimensional
massive
(MKG) on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+d}(d\geq 4)
in the Coulomb gauge. See also
[
30
,
13
]
and
[
12
,
11
]
for the asymptotic behavior of the (1+3) dimensional
massive
(MKG) and wave-Klein-Gordon type systems, respectively.

1.3.
Main Theorem

To identify the asymptotic behavior for Dirac spinor, we introduce Dirac projection operator and decompose spinor by using this projection. Let us denote
\left\langle D\right\rangle:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle)
and
|D|:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(|\xi|)
, where
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{-1}
are Fourier transform and its inverse, respectively, and
\left\langle\,\cdot\,\right\rangle:=(1+|\cdot|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
Then we define projection operators
\Pi_{\theta}
for
\theta\in\{+,-\}

by

\displaystyle\Pi_{\theta}\bigg(\!\!=\Pi_{\theta}(D)\bigg):=\frac{1}{2}\left(I_{4}+\theta\frac{\alpha\cdot D+{\beta}}{\left\langle D\right\rangle}\right).

(1.4)

We denote the symbol of
\Pi_{\theta}
by
\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)
. This simply implies
\psi=\Pi_{+}\psi+\Pi_{-}\psi
.

Let us define the standard vector fields as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\,(\mu=0,1,2,3),\quad{\Omega}_{jk}:=x_{j}\partial_{k}-x_{k}\partial_{j}\,(jk\in\{23,31,12\}),\quad{\Gamma}_{j}:=t\partial_{j}+x_{j}\partial_{t}\,(j=1,2,3),

which are the infinitesimal generators of translation, rotation, and Lorentz boost, respectively. For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we define sets of differential operators by

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{0}:=\{I\}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\mathcal{V}_{n}:=\left\{[\partial]^{a}[{\Omega}]^{b}[{\Gamma}]^{c}:|a|+|b|+|c|\leq n\right\},

where
a=(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\,b=(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),c=(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3})
,
|a|=\sum_{\mu=0}^{4}a_{\mu}
,
|b|=\sum_{j=1}^{3}b_{j}
,
|c|=\sum_{j=1}^{3}c_{j}
, and

\displaystyle[\partial]^{a}:=\partial_{0}^{a_{0}}\partial_{1}^{a_{1}}\partial_{2}^{a_{2}}\partial_{3}^{a_{3}},\quad[{\Omega}]^{b}:={\Omega}_{23}^{b_{1}}{\Omega}_{31}^{b_{2}}{\Omega}_{12}^{b_{3}},\quad[{\Gamma}]^{c}:={\Gamma}_{1}^{c_{1}}{\Gamma}_{2}^{c_{2}}{\Gamma}_{1}^{c_{2}}.

Our main theorem is stated as follows:

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
N(n)
be indexed as follows:

n

0

1

2

3

N(n)

70

30

20

10

Them, there exist
{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
such that:

(i)

Suppose that the initial data (
1.3
) satisfies that

\displaystyle\|\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\psi_{0}}\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}+\sum_{n=0}^{3}\left[\|\left\langle x\right\rangle^{n}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{n}\psi_{0}\|_{H^{N(n)}}+\|\left\langle x\right\rangle^{n}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{n}|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}(a_{\mu},\dot{a}_{\mu})\|_{H^{N(n)}}\right]<{\varepsilon}_{0}.

(1.5)

Then there exists a unique global solution
(\psi,A_{\mu})
with
(\psi,|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{\mu})\in C([0,\infty);H^{N(0)})
to (
MD
) in the Lorenz gauge (
1.1
).

(ii)

The solution
(\psi(t),A_{\mu}(t))
has the following asymptotic behavior: There exists
(\psi^{\infty}(t),A_{\mu}^{\infty}(t))
and phase modification
B_{\theta}(t,D)(\theta\in\{+,-\})
such that for some
0<{\delta},\zeta\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\|\Pi_{\theta}\psi(t)-e^{iB_{\theta}(t,D)}\Pi_{\theta}\psi^{\infty}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
\left\|(A_{\mu}(t),\partial_{t}A_{\mu}(t))-(A_{\mu}^{\infty}(t),\partial_{t}A_{\mu}^{\infty}(t))\right\|_{{\dot{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}+\zeta}\times{\dot{H}}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\zeta}}\end{array}\right\}=O(t^{-{\delta}})\;\;\mbox{as}\;\;t\to\infty,

where the phase modification is defined by the symbol

\displaystyle B_{\theta}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\Pi_{\theta}(\xi){\alpha}^{\mu}\int_{0}^{t}(P_{\leq K}A_{\mu})\left(s,\theta\frac{s\xi}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}\right)ds

(1.8)

for
K=K(s)
which is the largest integer such that
2^{K}\leq\left\langle s\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}-2\zeta}
,
and
(\psi^{\infty},A_{\mu}^{\infty})\in C(\mathbb{R};L^{2}\times\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+\zeta})
is a solution to the linear system (
MD
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} i{\alpha}^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\psi^{\infty}&=m\beta\psi^{\infty},\\
\square A_{\mu}^{\infty}&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(iii)

For some small
\overline{{\delta}}>0
, the solution
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies the energy bounds with slow growth,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{3}\sup_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|\mathcal{L}\Pi_{\theta}\psi(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}+\||D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}A_{\mu}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\overline{{\delta}}},

for
t\in[0,\infty)
. Moreover,
(\psi,A_{\mu})
decays as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{3}\sup_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|\mathcal{L}\Pi_{\theta}\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathcal{L}A_{\mu}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+\overline{{\delta}}}.

(1.9)

The novelty of this paper is to provide an explicit form of interaction between spinor and gauge fields resulting in modified scattering. The proof is based on the space-time resonance argument developed by Germain-Masmoudi-Shatah
[
18
,
20
,
19
]
and the vector-field energy method by Ionescu-Pausader
[
28
]
. We believe that our method for modified scattering used in this paper has its own specific advantages that would be of independent interest in various applications.
It remains still open to obtain the global existence and asymptotic behavior of (
MD
) in the other gauges (especially the Coulomb gauge). The Maxwell part consists of elliptic and wave equations in the Coulomb gauge. Due to the lack of Lorentz invariance in the elliptic equation, the extension of current results to the problem in the Coulomb gauge will be nontrivial. This will be addressed as a next issue.

Remark 1.2
.

(1)
The proof of Theorem
1.1
relies on a bootstrap argument under the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). The initial data condition (
1.5
) is essential for satisfying these assumptions. Specifically, the Fourier amplitude condition in (
1.5
) is crucial to demonstrate modified scattering behavior for the Dirac component.

(2)
The phase correction
B_{\theta}(t,\xi)
is a real-valued and exhibits
t^{\overline{{\delta}}}
-growth due to the slow decay (
1.9
) of the gauge fields (see (
8.33
)). In view of the dispersive effects, even if we obtain the full decay
t^{-1}
of the Maxwell part, this phase correction still possesses at least logarithmic divergence.

(3)
In (
(ii)
), this asymptotic behavior for Dirac spinor implies that there exists
B(t,D)
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi(t)-e^{iB(t,D)}\psi^{\infty}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\xrightarrow{t\to\infty}0.

Here,
B(t,D)
can be defined as

\displaystyle e^{iB(t,D)}:=e^{iB_{+}(t,D)}\Pi_{+}+e^{iB_{-}(t,D)}\Pi_{-}.

(4)
Theorem
1.1
describes the linear scattering behavior for the wave part, while the spinor component exhibits modified scattering effects. The regularity of the homogeneous space plays a role of a null structure, which implies additional time decay. Based on this observation, we also obtain

\displaystyle\left\|(A_{\mu}(t),\partial_{t}A_{\mu}(t))-(A_{\mu}^{\infty}(t),\partial_{t}A_{\mu}^{\infty}(t))\right\|_{{\dot{H}}^{m}\times{\dot{H}}^{m-1}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-{\delta}},

(1.10)

for
m\in[1,N(2)]
.

(5)
Within the existence time the solution has the same regularity as the initial data. We brief on this in Section
5
.

(6)
Some exponents appearing in Theorem
1.1
are obtained roughly. For instance, the regularity exponent
N(n)
could be improved. In this paper, we set
{\delta}=10^{-10}
,
\zeta=1050{\delta}
, and
\overline{{\delta}}=410{\delta}
and we will not pursue the sharpness of these parameters.

(7)
We tried to find the minimal order of
\mathcal{V}_{n}(n\in\{0,1,\cdots,n_{0}\})
, and
n_{0}=3
seems to be best in our analysis. One may consider higher orders
n_{0}\geq 4
by adjusting
N(n)
and
{\delta}
suitably. In this higher order case, one may get
t^{-\frac{3}{2}+\overline{{\delta}}}
decay for Dirac part.

1.4.
Main idea

We prove the existence of global solution and the asymptotic behavior with a bootstrap argument starting from an a priori assumption with energy and weighted energy estimates, which are equipped with differential operators
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
and scattering norms that we devise to control Fourier amplitude throughout the paper (see (
3.3
)–(
3.5
)). Our argument is summarized as the vector-field energy method, which enables us to handle the higher-order energy estimates by the normal form approach exploiting space-time resonances suitably.

The nonlinear terms of (
1.2
) give rise to several oscillatory integrals with the stationary points of the phase interactions. Our analysis begins with addressing the space, time, and space-time resonances of phase functions originating from the Dirac and wave propagators. Specifically, through a standard reformulation with the Dirac projection operator and first-order wave equations (see Section
2.1
below), we obtain the nonlinearities
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\Theta}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\mu,\Theta^{\prime}}
for the profiles
\phi_{\theta_{0}}=e^{{\theta_{0}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\psi_{{\theta_{0}}}
and
V_{\mu,{\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}=|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-{\theta_{0}}^{\prime}it|D|}A_{\mu,{\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}
, respectively:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}}e^{is\,p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\Pi_{\theta_{0}}(\xi)|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{V_{\mu,\theta_{2}}}(s,\eta)\alpha^{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\,d\eta ds,\;\;p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)=\theta\left\langle\xi\right\rangle-\theta_{1}\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\theta_{2}|\eta|,\\
&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+3}}|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{isq_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)}\left\langle\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}}(s,\eta),\alpha_{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{2}^{\prime}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\,d\eta ds,\;\;q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)=-\theta^{\prime}|\xi|+\theta^{\prime}_{1}\left\langle\eta\right\rangle-\theta^{\prime}_{2}\left\langle\xi+\eta\right\rangle,\end{aligned}

(1.11)

where 3-tuples
\Theta=(\theta,{\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}})
and
\Theta^{\prime}=(\theta^{\prime},{\theta_{1}}^{\prime},{\theta_{2}}^{\prime})
where
{\theta_{j}},{\theta_{j}}^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}\,(j=1,2)
. We call the functions
p_{\Theta}
and
q_{\Theta^{\prime}}

phase interactions
in this paper. As we can observe obviously, the nonlinearity of the Dirac part and wave part consists of Dirac-wave interaction and Dirac-Dirac interaction, respectively. Hence the phase interactions exhibit various sign relations, requiring consideration of different resonance cases.

While the phases experience space-time resonance, the nonlinearities of (
1.2
) lack sufficient null structures to eliminate these resonances. As observed in
[
8
,
27
,
16
,
6
,
33
]
, the relation between projection operators and Dirac matrices
{\alpha}_{j}
induces sign-changed projection parts and Riesz transform parts:

\displaystyle{\alpha}^{j}\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)=\Pi_{-\theta}(\xi){\alpha}^{j}+\theta\frac{\xi_{j}}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}.

(1.12)

Depending on the sigh relation, some terms with null structures emerge, whereas the interaction generates terms without null structures, regardless of the sign relation. To address the lack of null structure, we use a feature of Lorentz invariance in the Lorenz gauge. More precisely, we exploit the vector-field energy method alongside the space-time resonance argument. It is crucial to introduce the vector fields in the energy estimates in order to avoid the time growth, which is a cost of weighted estimates occurring in the space-time resonance estimates under the a priori assumptions. In fact, the number of weights implies the same number of time growth in the Duhamel’s formula. However, by imposing the vector fields in both energy and weighted estimates, we can convert weighted estimates into nonlinear estimates without additional time growth arising from Lorentz boosts (see (
2.15
) and (
2.17
)). Since the Dirac operator does not commute with Lorentz boots in contrast to the Klein-Gordon equation, we find out the exact additional terms coming from the commutator between vector fields and Dirac operators (see Section
2.3
). It turns out that the additional terms have no harm in the energy estimates. These terms arising in the exchange procedure can be decomposed into two parts. One part comprises vector fields of lower order, while the other part includes at least one time-translation vector field. In particular, the latter does not involve Lorentz boosts. Therefore, we can treat these terms as an easier case due to the lower order of vector fields throughout the entire estimates.

To show the asymptotic behavior of spinor, we need to control the scattering norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{D}}
as defined in (
3.1
). At the same time, a phase modification is required. In view of the dispersive estimates for Dirac and Maxwell equations, the nonlinearities are not integrable in time due to the resonance in the infrared regime. Hence the phase modification is necessary to get rid of the resonance effect of slow decay of the low frequency part of gauge fields
A_{\mu}
associated with the spinor. Indeed, the phase interaction of Dirac part has a decomposition
p_{\Theta}=p_{res}+p_{non}
, in which
p_{res}=\theta\frac{s\xi}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}
(when
\theta=\theta_{1})
has the most strong resonance and
p_{non}
can give a suitable decay effect (see (
8.8
)). By cutting off the low frequency part from
A_{\mu}
as stated in the main theorem, we will see that

\int_{0}^{t}\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\,\Theta}(t,\xi)-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{\theta is\,\frac{\xi\cdot\eta}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}}\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)\widehat{P_{\leq K}A_{\mu}}(s,\eta)\alpha^{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta}}(s,\xi)\,d\eta\right]ds=O(t^{-{\delta}})\;\;(t\to\infty).

This implies that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi_{\theta}}(t,\xi)=i\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)(P_{\leq K}A_{\mu})\left(t,\theta\frac{t\xi}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}\right)\alpha^{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta}}(t,\xi)+[\mbox{lower order terms}].

From this idea we construct the phase correction (
1.8
) and learn how to define the scattering norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{D}}
. For more diverse phase corrections, we refer to
[
10
,
22
,
24
]
.

Compared to the asymptotic behavior of Dirac spinor, Maxwell part exhibits the linear scattering feature via the scattering norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{M}}
. In view of (
1.11
), the obstacle preventing the dispersion effect for scattering is a singularity of the factor
|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Moreover, another obstacle is the space-time resonance of the phase appearing in (
1.11
) when
\xi=0
. Fortunately, these obstacles can be eliminated by the norm of homogeneous Sobolev space
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+\zeta}
for some
\zeta>0
. As a consequence we get the anticipated linear scattering like (
(ii)
) and (
1.10
) for the Maxwell part.

1.5.
Organization of paper

This paper is organized as follows. In Section 2 the half-Klein-Gordon and half-wave equations of (
1.2
) are derived via projections and also a system of profiles is considered. We classify the resonance sets of phase interactions. Then in order to describe the fields and profiles with differential operators, we look into the commutator
[\mathcal{L},\Pi_{\theta}]
for
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
, which generates nonlinear terms of smooth differential operators essentially in
\mathcal{V}_{n}
. In Section 3 we provide a bootstrap argument. For this purpose, we define target solution spaces defined by the energy of fields and weighted profiles equipped with differential operators
\mathcal{L}
, and to close bootstrapping we define control norms through the phase-space localization. Then we establish linear estimates under bootstrapping assumptions, which consists of estimates on the time decay and on the localized profile. In Section 4 we consider nonlinear estimates based on the bootstrapping assumptions and linear estimates established in Section 3. Since the nonlinear estimates are carried out in
L^{2}
space, the nonlinear terms generated by commutator turn out be harmless throughout the whole estimates. In Section 5 we prove the main theorem by assuming bootstrap argument. The Section 6 is devoted to proving the energy estimate parts of bootstrap and the Section 7 to proving the estimates of weighted profiles. In Section 8, 9 we show the asymptotic behaviors of spinor field and gauge field, respectively, from which we can control the scattering norms and close the bootstrap argument. We describe how to extract the phase modification of the Dirac spinor from the resonance interaction between spinor and gauge fields in Section 8. We also show the linear scattering results for gauge fields in Section 9.

1.6.
Notations

(1)

(Mixed-normed spaces) For a Banach space
X
and an interval
I
,
u\in L_{I}^{q}X
iff
u(t)\in X
for a.e.
t\in I
and
\|u\|_{L_{I}^{q}X}:=\|\|u(t)\|_{X}\|_{L_{I}^{q}}<\infty
. Especially, we abbreviate
L^{p}=L_{x}^{p}
for the spatial norm and indicate the subscripts for only Fourier space norm.

(2)

(Sobolev spaces) Let
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
. We define homogeneous Sobolev spaces
\|u\|_{{\dot{H}}^{s}}:=\||D|^{s}u\|_{L^{2}}
. For inhomogeneous spaces, we define
\|u\|_{H^{s}}:=\|\left\langle D\right\rangle^{s}u\|_{L^{2}}
.

(3)

Different positive constants depending only on
n
,
{\varepsilon}_{0}
are denoted by the same letter
C
, if not specified.
A\lesssim B
and

A\gtrsim B
mean that
A\leq CB
and
A\geq C^{-1}B
respectively for some
C>0
.
A\sim B
means that
A\lesssim B
and
A\gtrsim B
.

(4)

(Littlewood-Paley operators) Let
\rho
be a
Littlewood-Paley function such that
\rho\in C_{0}^{\infty}(B(0,2))

with
\rho(\xi)=1
for
|\xi|\leq 1
and define
\rho_{k}(\xi):=\rho\left(\frac{\xi}{2^{k}}\right)-\rho\left(\frac{\xi}{2^{k-1}}\right)

for
2\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then we define the frequency projection

P_{k}
by
\mathcal{F}(P_{k}f)(\xi)=\rho_{k}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)
,
and also
\rho_{\leq k}:=\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\rho_{k^{\prime}}
and
\rho_{>k}=1-\rho_{\leq k}
. Then we also denote
\mathcal{F}(P_{\leq k}f)(\xi)=\rho_{\leq k}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)
and
\mathcal{F}(P_{>k}f)(\xi)=\rho_{>k}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)
. For
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}

we denote
\rho_{[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]}=\sum_{\ell_{1}\leq k\leq\ell_{2}}\rho_{k}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{k}:=\rho_{[k-2,k+2]}
. In
particular,
\widetilde{P_{k}}P_{k}=P_{k}\widetilde{P_{k}}=P_{k}

where
\widetilde{P_{k}}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\widetilde{\rho_{k}}\mathcal{F}
.

(5)

(Localization on phase space) Let
\mathcal{U}_{k}=\{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}:k+j\geq 0\,(k\leq 0)\mbox{ and }j=0\,(k\geq 1)\}
for fixed
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
means
j\geq-\min(k,0)
. For any
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
, let

\bar{\rho}^{(k)}_{j}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\rho_{\leq-k}(x)&\mbox{if}\;k+j=0\;\mbox{and}\;k\leq 0,\\
\rho_{\leq 0}(x)&\mbox{if}\;j=0\;\mbox{and}\;k\geq 1,\\
\rho_{j}(x)&\mbox{if}\;k+j\geq 1\;\mbox{and}\;j\geq 1.\end{array}\right.

Then,
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\bar{\rho}_{j}^{(k)}=1
. For
k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\,j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
, let
Q_{jk}
denote the phase space localization operator

Q_{jk}f(x)=\bar{\rho}_{j}^{(k)}(x)P_{k}f(x).

We note that the uncertainty principle allows us to consider
Q_{jk}
only when
2^{j+k}\geq 1
.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Set up for the Maxwell-Dirac system

By the definition of Dirac projection (
1.4
),
\Pi_{\theta}
satisfies the properties:

\Pi_{\theta}+\Pi_{-\theta}=I_{4},\quad\Pi_{\theta}\Pi_{-\theta}=0,\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\Pi_{\theta}\Pi_{\theta}=\Pi_{\theta}.

We can decompose the Dirac spinor
\psi
into half Klein-Gordon waves, i.e.,
\psi_{+}
and
\psi_{-}
by projection operator
\Pi_{\theta}
.
Let
\psi_{\theta}=\Pi_{\theta}\psi
for
\theta\in\{+,-\}
. Then we obtain the following decoupled equations from the Dirac part of (
1.2
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} (-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle})\psi_{\theta}&=\Pi_{\theta}\big(A_{\mu}\alpha^{\mu}\psi\big),\\
\psi_{\theta}(0)&:=\psi_{0,\theta}.\end{aligned}\right.

Note that
\psi=\psi_{+}+\psi_{-}
.

We now decompose gauge field as
A_{\mu}=A_{\mu,+}+A_{\mu,-}
with

A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(1+\theta^{\prime}|D|^{-1}(-i\partial_{t})\Big)A_{\mu},

for
\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
. Then the Maxwell part of (
1.2
) is rewritten as the following equations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} (i\partial_{t}+\theta^{\prime}|D|)A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}&=\theta^{\prime}\frac{1}{2}|D|^{-1}\left\langle\psi,\alpha_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle,\\
A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(0)&:=a_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}:=\frac{1}{2}(a_{\mu}-\theta^{\prime}i|D|^{-1}\dot{a}_{\mu}).\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore (
1.2
) becomes the system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} (-i\partial_{t}+\theta\left\langle D\right\rangle)\psi_{\theta}&=\Pi_{\theta}\big(A_{\mu}\alpha^{\mu}\psi\big),\\
(i\partial_{t}+\theta^{\prime}|D|)A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}&=\theta^{\prime}\frac{1}{2}|D|^{-1}\left\langle\psi,\alpha_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle\end{aligned}\right.

with initial data
(\psi_{\theta}(0),A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(0))=(\psi_{0,\theta},a_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}})
, for
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
. Defining
W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}:=|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}
(
W_{\mu}:=W_{\mu,+}+W_{\mu,-}
), we also have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} (-i\partial_{t}+\theta\left\langle D\right\rangle)\psi_{\theta}&=\Pi_{\theta}\left[\left(|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}W_{\mu}\right)\alpha^{\mu}\psi\right],\\
(i\partial_{t}+\theta^{\prime}|D|)W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}&=\theta^{\prime}\frac{1}{2}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\psi,\alpha_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle.\end{aligned}\right.

(2.1)

Now by Duhamel’s principle, (
2.1
) can be converted into

\displaystyle\psi_{\theta}(t)

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\psi_{0,\theta}+i\sum_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\{\pm\}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\theta i(t-s){\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\Pi_{\theta}\left(\left(|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}W_{\mu,\theta_{2}}\right)\alpha^{\mu}\psi_{\theta_{1}}\right)(s)\,ds,

\displaystyle W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t)

\displaystyle=e^{\theta^{\prime}it|D|}|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}-\theta^{\prime}\frac{i}{2}\sum_{\theta^{\prime}_{1},\theta^{\prime}_{2}\in\{\pm\}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\theta^{\prime}i(t-s)|D|}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\psi_{\theta^{\prime}_{1}},\alpha_{\mu}\psi_{\theta^{\prime}_{2}}\right\rangle(s)\,ds.

To keep track of the scattering state, we define profile fields
\phi_{\theta}
and
V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}
by

\displaystyle\phi_{\theta}(t):=e^{\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\psi_{\theta}(t)\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t):=e^{-\theta^{\prime}it|D|}W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t).

By taking Fourier transform and time derivative, one gets the frequency representation as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}_{\theta}(t,\xi)&=\frac{i}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\{\pm\}}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\,\Theta}(t,\xi)\;\;(\Theta=(\theta,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})),\\
\partial_{t}\widehat{V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}}(t,\xi)&=-\theta^{\prime}\frac{i}{2(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\theta^{\prime}_{1},\theta^{\prime}_{2}\in\{\pm\}}\mathbf{V}_{\,\mu,\Theta^{\prime}}(t,\xi)\;\;(\Theta^{\prime}=(\theta^{\prime},\theta_{1}^{\prime},\theta_{2}^{\prime})),\end{aligned}

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Phi}_{\,\Theta}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\,p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{V_{\mu,\theta_{2}}}(s,\eta)\alpha^{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\,d\eta,

(2.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{\,\mu,\Theta^{\prime}}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle:=|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{isq_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)}\left\langle\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}}(s,\eta),\alpha_{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{2}^{\prime}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\,d\eta,

(2.3)

\displaystyle p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\theta\left\langle\xi\right\rangle-\theta_{1}\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\theta_{2}|\eta|,

(2.4)

\displaystyle q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle:=-\theta^{\prime}|\xi|+\theta^{\prime}_{1}\left\langle\eta\right\rangle-\theta^{\prime}_{2}\left\langle\xi+\eta\right\rangle.

(2.5)

Remark 2.1
.

Our energy estimates rely on the quasilinear characteristics of (
MD
), rather than directly utilizing the specific forms given in (
2.2
) and (
2.3
). However, from the significance of observing the oscillations within the nonlinearities of (
MD
), we include the forms(
2.2
) and (
2.3
) for convenience.

2.2.
Space-time resonance for Maxwell-Dirac system

We proceed our proof by a bootstrap argument based on the space-time resonance argument

[
18
,
19
,
20
]
(see also
[
23
]
). In this section we investigate time, space, and space-time resonances for
massive
Maxwell-Dirac system, respectively. These types of resonance have been already investigated in
[
33
]
. For the readers’ convenience, we mention them again in this section. For the time resonant set of wave and Klein-Gordon type systems, we refer to
[
28
,
29
]
. We also refer to
[
40
,
4
]
for the analogous observation of Dirac operator. According to the definition in
[
18
]
, we define time, space, and space-time resonant sets as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{T}_{p_{\Theta}}&:=\left\{(\xi,\eta):p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)=0\right\},\\
\mathcal{S}_{p_{\Theta}}&:=\left\{(\xi,\eta):\nabla_{\eta}p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)=0\right\},\\
\mathcal{R}_{p_{\Theta}}&:=\mathcal{T}_{p_{\Theta}}\cap\mathcal{S}_{p_{\Theta}},\end{aligned}\hskip 56.9055pt\begin{aligned} \mathcal{T}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}&:=\left\{(\xi,\eta):q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)=0\right\},\\
\mathcal{S}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}&:=\left\{(\xi,\eta):\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)=0\right\},\\
\mathcal{R}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}&:=\mathcal{T}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}\cap\mathcal{S}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}.\end{aligned}

To identify these sets, it suffices to examine the lower bounds of phase interactions
p_{\Theta},q_{\Theta^{\prime}}
and their gradients
\nabla_{\eta}p_{\Theta},\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta^{\prime}}
.

Let us observe that
p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)
never vanishes for any sign
\theta,{\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}\in\{+,-\}
, when
\xi\neq 0
and
\eta\neq 0
. In fact, if for some
\xi,\eta
,
p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)=0
, then

(\theta\left\langle\xi\right\rangle+\theta_{2}|\eta|)^{2}=1+|\xi-\eta|^{2}.

This implies that

{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}|\eta|=|\xi\cdot\eta|\;\;\mbox{and hence}\;\;{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}\leq|\xi|,

which is a contradiction. Hence we have

\displaystyle|p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|

\displaystyle=\left|\theta{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}-\theta_{1}\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\theta_{2}|\eta|\right|=\left|\frac{(\theta{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}+\theta_{2}|\eta|)^{2}-\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle^{2}}{\theta{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}+\theta_{1}\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\theta_{2}|\eta|}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2\theta\theta_{2}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}|\eta|+2\xi\cdot\eta}{\theta{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}+\theta_{1}\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\theta_{2}|\eta|}\right|.

If
\theta=\theta_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|&\geq\frac{2|\eta|({\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}-|\xi|)}{{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}+\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+|\eta|}\gtrsim\frac{|\eta|}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle+\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\left\langle\eta\right\rangle)}.\end{aligned}

(2.6)

If
\theta\neq\theta_{1},\theta\neq\theta_{2}
, then

\displaystyle|p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|\geq\frac{2|\eta|({\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}-|\xi|)}{|{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}-\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle-|\eta||}\gtrsim({\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}-|\xi|)\gtrsim\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-1}.

Also if
\theta\neq\theta_{1},\theta=\theta_{2}
, then trivially
|p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|\geq\left\langle\xi\right\rangle+\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle
. Then we have, for
{\theta_{0}}\neq{\theta_{1}}
,

\displaystyle|p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-1}.

Therefore
\mathcal{T}_{p_{\Theta}}=\{\eta=0\}
when
{\theta_{0}}={\theta_{1}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{p_{\Theta}}=\emptyset
, otherwise.

Now one can readily observe that there is no space resonance of (
2.2
) due to the fact

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{\eta}p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)\right|=\left|{\theta_{1}}\frac{\xi-\eta}{\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}-{\theta_{2}}\frac{\eta}{|\eta|}\right|\gtrsim 1-\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}\gtrsim\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle^{-2}.

(2.7)

Therefore
\mathcal{S}_{p_{\Theta}}=\emptyset
for any sign and hence
\mathcal{R}_{p_{\Theta}}=\emptyset
.

By virtue of the similarity between
p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)
and
q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)
, we readily see that

\displaystyle|q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim\begin{cases}\frac{|\xi|}{\left\langle\eta\right\rangle(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle+\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+\left\langle\eta\right\rangle)}&\mbox{ if }\theta_{1}^{\prime}=\theta_{2}^{\prime},\\
\left\langle\eta\right\rangle^{-1}&\mbox{ otherwise},\end{cases}

(2.8)

and a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim\begin{cases}\frac{|\xi|}{\left\langle\xi+\eta\right\rangle^{3}}&\mbox{ if }\theta_{1}^{\prime}=\theta_{2}^{\prime},\\
\left|\frac{\eta}{\left\langle\eta\right\rangle}+\frac{\xi+\eta}{\left\langle\xi+\eta\right\rangle}\right|&\mbox{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(2.9)

Note that space resonance of
q_{\Theta^{\prime}}
is different from that of
p_{\Theta}
. Therefore, we have
\mathcal{T}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}=\mathcal{S}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}=\mathcal{R}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}=\{\xi=0\}
when
\theta_{1}^{\prime}=\theta_{2}^{\prime}
.
On the other hand, if
\theta_{1}^{\prime}\neq\theta_{2}^{\prime}
, then

\mathcal{T}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{S}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}=\{\xi=-2\eta\}
, and
\mathcal{R}_{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}}=\emptyset
.

2.3.
Vector fields on Dirac and Maxwell parts

By the Lorentz invariance the wave operator
\square
commutes with
\mathcal{L}
for any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
. Hence

\displaystyle-\square\mathcal{L}W_{\mu}=|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle.

(2.10)

On the other hand, the vector fields of rotations and boosts do not commute with both of the half Klein-Gordon operators
-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle}
and projection operators
\Pi_{\theta}
, we need to investigate carefully the commutators between vector fields and those operators.

Rotations.
The vector fields
\Omega
commute with the half Klein-Gordon operator. Indeed,

\displaystyle(-i\partial_{t}+\theta\left\langle D\right\rangle)\Omega_{jk}\psi_{\theta}=\Omega_{jk}\Pi_{\theta}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi).

On the other hand,
{\Omega}
does not commute with the projection operators and the commutator is the following:

\displaystyle\left[{\Omega}_{jk},\Pi_{\theta}\right]=\theta\frac{i}{2}\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{j}\partial_{k}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}-\frac{{\alpha}_{k}\partial_{j}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\right).

(2.11)

Lorentz boosts.
The Lorentz vector field
\Gamma
does not commute with half-Klein-Gordon operators and their commutators are as follows:

\displaystyle(-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle})\Gamma_{j}\psi_{\theta}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j}(-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle})\psi_{\theta}-i\partial_{j}\psi_{\theta}-\theta\frac{\partial_{j}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\partial_{t}\psi_{\theta}.

The first formula implies that

\displaystyle(-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle}){\Gamma}_{j}\psi_{\theta}

\displaystyle={\Gamma}_{j}\Pi_{\theta}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)-\theta\frac{\partial_{j}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\Pi_{\theta}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi).

A direct calculation shows that
for
j=1,2,3
,

\displaystyle[{\Gamma}_{j},\Pi_{\theta}]=\theta\frac{i}{2}\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{j}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}+i\frac{({\alpha}\cdot D)\partial_{j}+m\beta\partial_{j}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}^{3}}\right)\partial_{t}.

(2.12)

Then for any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
it follows from (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle})\mathcal{L}\psi_{\theta}&=\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)+[\mathcal{L},\Pi_{\theta}](A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)\\
&=\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)+\sum_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi).\end{aligned}

(2.13)

Here,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
are the operator-valued coefficients bounded in
L^{p}(1<p<\infty)
of the form
R^{c}
, where
C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
is a
4\times 4
matrix defined by multiplications of
{\alpha}^{\mu}
and
\beta
,
c=(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3})
,
R^{c}=R_{1}^{c_{1}}R_{2}^{c_{2}}R_{3}^{c_{3}}
, and
R_{j}
are Riesz type transforms defined by
\frac{-i\partial_{j}}{{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}
. The coefficients
C
and multi-indices
c
may vary in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
. In fact, let us denote
\mathcal{O}_{jk}:=\theta\left[{\Omega}_{jk},\Pi_{\theta}\right]
and
\mathcal{G}_{j}\partial_{t}:=\theta[{\Gamma}_{j},\Pi_{\theta}]
. Then
for any
\mathcal{L}=[\partial]^{a}[{\Omega}]^{b}[{\Gamma}]^{c}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
, the commutator can be written as

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L},\Pi_{\theta}]=

\displaystyle\theta[\partial]^{a}[{\Omega}]^{b}[\mathcal{G}]^{c}\partial_{0}^{|c|}+\theta[\partial]^{a}[\mathcal{O}]^{b}[{\Gamma}]^{c}-\theta[\mathcal{G}]^{c}\partial_{0}^{|c|}+\theta\sum_{j=1}^{3}c_{j}[\partial]^{a}[\mathcal{O}]^{b}R_{j}^{c_{j}},

where
[\mathcal{O}]^{b}=\mathcal{O}_{23}^{b_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{31}^{b_{2}}\mathcal{O}_{12}^{b_{3}}
and
[\mathcal{G}]^{c}=\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c_{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c_{3}}
.
Rearranging four terms in order of
\partial,{\Omega},{\Gamma}
, one can get the expression
C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})R^{c}
. Especially, we note that, for
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
, there exists
\overline{\mathcal{L}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{n}}
such that
n_{0},\overline{n}\leq n-1
and

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L},\Pi_{\theta}]=\mathcal{L}_{0}+\sum_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{\overline{n}}}C(\overline{\mathcal{L}})\mathcal{R}_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}\partial_{t},

(2.14)

where

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{0}:=\mathcal{V}_{0}\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{\overline{n}}:=\{[\partial]^{a}[{\Omega}]^{b}:|a|+|b|\leq\overline{n}\}.

Remark 2.2
.

In (
2.14
),
\mathcal{L}_{0}
consists of lower order vector fields compared to
\mathcal{L}
. Similarly,
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
in the second term of the right-hand side of (
2.14
) also comprises lower order vector fields. As well as, the Lorentz vector field
{\Gamma}
is not included in
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
. This fact implies that
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
commutes not only with
\partial_{t}
, but also with the differential operators
|D|
and
\left\langle D\right\rangle
.

Throughout the paper we use the following notations for the fields and profiles equipped with vector fields: For
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}:=\mathcal{L}\psi_{\theta},\quad\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}:=e^{\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}},

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}}:=\mathcal{L}A_{\mu},\quad A_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}:=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\theta^{\prime}\frac{-i\partial_{t}}{|D|}\right)A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}},

\displaystyle W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}}:=|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}},\quad W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}:=|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}},\quad V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}:=e^{-\theta^{\prime}it|D|}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}.

The Lorentz boosts play the role of weight generators. Indeed, we observe

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\Gamma}_{j}\psi_{\theta}&=x_{j}(-\theta i{\left\langle D\right\rangle}\psi_{\theta}+i\Pi_{\theta}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi))+t\partial_{j}\psi_{\theta}\\
&=-\theta ie^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}x_{j}\left({\left\langle D\right\rangle}\phi_{\theta}\right)+ix_{j}\Pi_{\theta}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)\end{aligned}

(2.15)

for
j=1,2,3
. On the other hand, by (
2.1
) and (
2.10
), there holds

\displaystyle(i\partial_{t}+\theta^{\prime}|D|)W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}=\theta^{\prime}\frac{1}{2}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle.

(2.16)

Analogously to (
2.15
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\Gamma}_{j}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}&=x_{j}(\theta^{\prime}i|D|W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}-\theta^{\prime}i\frac{1}{2}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle)+t\partial_{j}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}\\
&=\theta^{\prime}ie^{\theta^{\prime}it|D|}x_{j}\left(|D|V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}\right)-\theta^{\prime}i\frac{1}{2}x_{j}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle.\end{aligned}

(2.17)

3.
Linear estimates

3.1.
A priori assumption

Here we define the solution spaces for the bootstrap argument. We also recall the regularity depending on the number of vector fields and set time growth index table as follows:

n

0

1

2

3

N(n)

70

30

20

10

H(n)

1

10

210

410

Let
{\varepsilon}_{1}:={\varepsilon}_{0}^{\frac{2}{3}}
for a small
{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
,
{\delta}=10^{-10}
. Then the scattering norms
\|\cdot\|_{\bf D},\|\cdot\|_{\bf M}
to control the bootstrap argument are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{{\bf D}}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{20}2^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{100})k}\|\rho_{k}\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{38}2^{-(1-\frac{1}{100})k}\|P_{k}\varphi\|_{L^{2}}\right\},

(3.1)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{{\bf M}}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{25}2^{(1+5H(2){\delta})k}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}2^{j}\|Q_{jk}v\|_{L^{2}}\right\}.

(3.2)

Given any
T>0
, assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
and that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,
n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
n_{1}\in\{0,1,2\}
,
l\in\{1,2,3\}
, and
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
, and the solution satisfies the bootstrap hypotheses

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\left(\left\|\psi_{\theta,{\mathcal{L}}}(t)\right\|_{H^{N(n)}}+\left\|W_{\mu,{\mathcal{L}},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{H^{N(n)}}\right)\leq{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n){\delta}},

(3.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sup_{\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{1}}}\sup_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n_{1}+1)}\left(\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle\left\|\rho_{k}(\xi)(\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}})(t,\xi)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}+2^{k/2}\left\|\rho_{k}(\xi)(\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}})(t,\xi)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\qquad\leq{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n_{1}+1){\delta}},\end{aligned}

(3.4)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{\theta}(t)\right\|_{{\bf D}}+\left\|V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{{\bf M}}\leq{\varepsilon}_{1}.

(3.5)

3.2.
Time decay estimates

In this section, we give the time decay properties of the Maxwell-Dirac system. To this end, we introduce some abbreviations as follows:

\displaystyle{\varphi}_{j,k}:=\widetilde{P}_{k}Q_{jk}{\varphi},\quad Q_{\leq jk}{\varphi}:=\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{k},j^{\prime}\leq j}Q_{j^{\prime}k}{\varphi},\quad{\varphi}_{\leq j,k}:=\widetilde{P}_{k}Q_{\leq jk}{\varphi}.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
{\varphi}\in L^{2}
and
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
for some
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then we get the following:

(i)

For any
{\alpha}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{+})^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{{{\varphi}_{j,k}}}\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{|{\alpha}|j}\left\|\widehat{Q_{jk}{\varphi}}\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}},\;\;\left\|\nabla_{\xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{{{\varphi}_{j,k}}}\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{|{\alpha}|j}\left\|\widehat{Q_{jk}{\varphi}}\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}},

(3.6)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{{{\varphi}_{j,k}}}\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left(2^{\frac{3j}{2}}\left\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}},2^{\frac{j}{2}-k}2^{\frac{{\delta}(j+k)}{8}}\left\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\right\|_{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}\right),

(3.7)

where
\|v\|_{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}=(\|\Omega_{12}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Omega_{23}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Omega_{31}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

(ii)

We have

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\|_{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}\leq A\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\sup_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}2^{j+k}\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\|_{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}\leq B,

for some
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
A\leq B\in[0,\infty)
, then we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{P_{k}{\varphi}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\frac{3k}{2}}A^{\frac{1-\zeta}{2}}B^{\frac{1+\zeta}{2}}

(3.8)

for any
\zeta\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

See (i) of Lemma 3.4 of
[
28
]
.
∎

Lemma 3.2

(Time decay for Dirac part)
.

Let
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\theta\in\{+,-\}
, and
{\varphi}\in L^{2}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}{\varphi}_{j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\min\left(2^{\frac{3k}{2}},\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{3}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{\frac{3j}{2}}\right)\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}

(3.9)

for
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
. Moreover, if
\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2}\ll 2^{2k}\left\langle t\right\rangle
and
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
, then we have

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}{\varphi}_{j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{5}2^{\frac{j}{2}-k}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\left\langle t\right\rangle 2^{2k})^{\frac{{\delta}}{8}}\left\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\right\|_{{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}}\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;2^{j}\ll 2^{k}\left\langle t\right\rangle

(3.10)

and also

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}{\varphi}_{\leq j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{5}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left\|\widehat{Q_{\leq jk}{\varphi}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;2^{j}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.11)

Proof.

See (iii) of Lemma 3.4 of
[
28
]
.
∎

Lemma 3.3

(Time decay for Maxwell part)
.

Let
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\theta\in\{+,-\}
, and
{\varphi}\in L^{2}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it|D|}{\varphi}_{j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\frac{3k}{2}}\min\left(1,2^{j}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}\right)\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}},

(3.12)

for
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
. Moreover, if
|t|\geq 1
and
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
, then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{[-100,100]}\left(\frac{x}{\left\langle t\right\rangle}\right)e^{-\theta it|D|}{\varphi}_{j,k}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{\frac{k}{2}}(1+\left\langle t\right\rangle 2^{k})^{\frac{{\delta}}{8}}\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\|_{{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}},

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it|D|}{\varphi}_{j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{\frac{k}{2}}(1+\left\langle t\right\rangle 2^{k})^{\frac{{\delta}}{8}}\left\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\right\|_{{H_{\Omega}^{0,1}}}\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;2^{j}\ll\left\langle t\right\rangle,

and

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it|D|}{\varphi}_{\leq j,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{2k}\left\|\widehat{Q_{\leq jk}{\varphi}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;2^{j}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}.

Proof.

See (ii) of Lemma 3.4 of
[
28
]
.
∎

3.3.
Universal tools

Lemma 3.4

(Coifman-Meyer operator estimates)
.

Let
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
satisfy that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
. Assume that a multiplier
m
satisfies

\|\textbf{m}\|_{\rm CM}:=\left\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3+3}}\mathbf{m}(\xi,\zeta)e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{iy\cdot\eta}d\xi d\eta\right\|_{L_{x,y}^{1}}\leq C_{\mathbf{m}}<\infty\quad(\zeta=\eta\;\,\mbox{or}\,\;\xi-\eta).

Then

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{m}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{v}(\eta)\widehat{w}(\xi-\eta)d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}\lesssim C_{\mathbf{m}}\|v\|_{L^{p}}\|w\|_{L^{q}}.

Lemma 3.5

(Lemma 3.5 of
[
28
]
)
.

Let
{\varphi}\in L^{2}
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then, for

\displaystyle A_{k}:=\|P_{k}{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left\|\rho_{k}(\xi)(\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{{\varphi}})(\xi)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}},\qquad B_{k}:=\left[\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}2^{2j}\|Q_{jk}{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}},

we have

\displaystyle A_{k}\lesssim\sum_{2^{k}\sim 2^{k^{\prime}}}B_{k^{\prime}}

and

\displaystyle B_{k}\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{2^{k}\sim 2^{k^{\prime}}}A_{k^{\prime}}&\mbox{ for }k\geq 0,\\
&\sum_{k^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{2}}\min(1,2^{k^{\prime}-k})A_{k^{\prime}}&\mbox{ for }k<0.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.13)

Especially, we have

\displaystyle 2^{-k}\|P_{k}{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{2}}\min(1,2^{k^{\prime}-k})A_{k^{\prime}}.

(3.14)

3.4.
Profile estimates

We define the localized profiles as follows:

\displaystyle\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{j,k}(t):=\widetilde{P}_{k}Q_{jk}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t),\qquad\quad\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\leq J,k}(t):=\sum_{j\leq J}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{j,k}(t),\qquad\quad\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{>J,k}(t):=\sum_{j>J}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{j,k}(t),

\displaystyle V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}^{j,k}(t):=\widetilde{P}_{k}Q_{jk}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t),\quad V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}^{\leq J,k}(t):=\sum_{j\leq J}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}^{j,k}(t),\quad\;\;V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}^{>J,k}(t):=\sum_{j>J}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}^{j,k}(t).

Lemma 3.6
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
and satisfies (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, and
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\|P_{k}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}G_{n}(t,k),

(3.15)

where

\displaystyle G_{n}(t,k):=\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

Moreover, if
n\leq 2,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
l\in\{1,2,3\}
, then

\displaystyle\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle\left\|\rho_{k}(\xi)\left(\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}\right)(t,\xi)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}+2^{\frac{k}{2}}\left\|\rho_{k}(\xi)\left(\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}}\right)(t,\xi)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}G_{n+1}(t,k),

(3.16)

As a consequence, if
n\leq 2
and
j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
, then

\displaystyle 2^{j}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle\left\|Q_{jk}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+2^{j}2^{k}\left\|Q_{jk}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}G_{n+1}(t,k)

(3.17)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle\left\|P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+2^{k}\left\|P_{k}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{k}G_{n+1}(t,k).

(3.18)

Proof.

By the a priori assumptions (
3.3
) and (
3.4
), we directly obtain (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), respectively. From (
3.5
) and (
3.13
), we also have (
3.17
). By (
3.17
) with the summation over
j
, the bound (
3.18
) holds.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
for some
T>1
and satisfies (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},n\in\{0,1,2\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi^{j,k}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}G_{n+1}(t,k)\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2}2^{\frac{k}{2}}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{2k}),

(3.19)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left\|e^{\theta^{\prime}it|D|}V^{j,k}_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{{\delta}}{2}}G_{n+1}(t,k)\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2}2^{\frac{k}{2}}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{k}),

(3.20)

for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Moreover, if
n\leq 1
and
\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}\ll 2^{2k}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{j}\in[2^{-k},2^{k}\left\langle t\right\rangle]}\left\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi^{j,k}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{{\delta}}{2}}G_{n+2}(t,k)\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{6}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}.

(3.21)

Proof.

By (
3.17
) and (
3.9
), we have (
3.19
). Similarly, the bound (
3.20
) follows from (
3.12
) and (
3.17
). Using (
3.17
), we see that, for
|b|\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|Q_{jk}{\Omega}^{b}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}G_{n+|b|+1}(t,k)\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-1}2^{-j}.

(3.22)

This together with (
3.10
) implies (
3.21
).
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
for some
T>1
and satisfies (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},n\in\{0,1,2\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi^{j,k}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\zeta}}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}G_{n+1}(t,k)\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2}\min\left(2^{(\frac{1}{2}-3\zeta){k}}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{\frac{5k}{2}}\right),

(3.23)

for
0<\zeta\ll 1
.

Proof.

By (
3.9
), we have

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{j,k}(t)\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\zeta}}}\lesssim\min\left(2^{\frac{3k}{2}-3\zeta k},2^{\frac{3j}{2}-3\zeta j}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{3-6\zeta}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}+3\zeta}\right)\|Q_{jk}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}.

Then, (
3.17
) implies (
3.23
) directly.
∎

Lemma 3.9

(Vector fields free estimates)
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
and satisfies (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}2^{j}\left\|Q_{jk}A_{\mu}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{-(1+5H(2){\delta})k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-25},\\
\left\|\widehat{{P_{k}}\psi_{\theta}}(t)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{100})k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-25},\\
\left\|{P_{k}}\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{(1+\frac{1}{100})k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+2}.\end{aligned}

(3.24)

We also get

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left\|e^{\theta it|D|}V^{j,k}_{\mu,\theta}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\min\left(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{k}\right)2^{\left(\frac{1}{2}-5H(2){\delta}\right)k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-22}.

(3.25)

Moreover, for
\left\langle t\right\rangle\gg 2^{-2k}
and
2^{J}\in[2^{-k},C2^{k}\left\langle t\right\rangle]
for some
C\ll 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\theta it\left\langle D\right\rangle}\phi_{\theta}^{\leq J,k}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{1000})k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-19}.

(3.26)

Proof.

The estimates in (
3.24
) follow from the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{D}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{M}}
. Then (
3.12
) and (
3.24
) imply (
3.25
). To prove (
3.26
), we consider the case
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{30}}
. The bound (
3.15
) yields (
3.26
) for
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{30}}
. If
2^{k}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{1000}}
or
2^{J}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}
, it also follows from (
3.24
) and (
3.11
). The remaining case is that
2^{k}\in[\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{1000}},\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{30}}]
and
2^{J}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}
. By (
3.10
) and (
3.22
), we estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{j}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|e^{-\theta it\left\langle D\right\rangle}\phi_{\theta}^{j,k}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{j}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-\frac{j}{2}-k}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{11{\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-26}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{1000})k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-19}.

This finishes the proof of (
3.26
).
∎

The following lemma will be used in Section
8
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
and satisfies (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
t\in[0,T]
, and
l\in\{1,2,3\}
. If
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}-10H(2){\delta}m}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}P_{k}(x_{l}V_{\mu,\theta})(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{8}}.

Proof.

Taking Fourier transform and then time derivative, one gets

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{P_{k}x_{l}V_{\mu,\theta}}(t)=\rho_{k}(\xi)\partial_{\xi_{l}}\left[|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\left\langle\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(t,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{2}}}(t,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\eta\right].

(3.27)

If the derivative
\partial_{\xi_{l}}
falls on
\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{j}}}
, the bound of (
3.27
) follows from (
3.16
). Thus, we consider the cases that the derivative falls on
|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
or the phase modulation
e^{itq_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}
. Let us first handle the case that the derivative falls on the phase modulation:

\displaystyle t\rho_{k}(\xi)|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\nabla_{\xi}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(t,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{2}}}(t,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\eta.

In view of (
2.8
) and (
2.9
), it suffices to consider only the case
{\theta_{1}}={\theta_{2}}
. By integration by parts in
\eta
, we get the following terms:

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(\xi)|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\nabla_{\eta}\frac{\nabla_{\xi}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}{|\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(t,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{2}}}(t,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\eta,

(3.28)

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(\xi)|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\nabla_{\xi}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}{|\nabla_{\eta}q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{x\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(t,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{2}}}(t,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\eta,

(3.29)

and additional symmetric term. To estimate (
3.28
), we make the dyadic decomposition for
|\eta|
and
|\xi+\eta|
into
2^{k_{1}}
and
2^{k_{2}}
, respectively. If
2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, then by (
3.18
) we have

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\eqref{eq:esti-wei-time-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}2^{-\frac{3k}{2}}2^{\frac{-2k_{2}+3\min(k_{1},k_{2})}{2}}\left\|{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{\theta_{1}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|{P_{k}}_{2}\phi_{\theta_{2}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}2^{-\frac{3k}{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2H(1){\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(k_{1},k_{2})}{2}+k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

On the other hand, we handle the case
2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. We decompose the profiles into
P_{k_{\ell}}\phi_{\theta_{j}}=\phi_{\theta_{\ell}}^{\leq J,k_{\ell}}+\phi_{\theta_{\ell}}^{>J,k_{\ell}}
for
\ell=1,2
with
2^{J}=C\left\langle t\right\rangle 2^{k_{\ell}}
for some
C\ll 1
, and we denote
\phi_{\theta_{\ell}}^{1}=\phi_{\theta_{\ell}}^{\leq J,k_{\ell}}
and
\phi_{\theta_{\ell}}^{2}=\phi_{\theta_{\ell}}^{>J,k_{\ell}}
for
a,b=1,2
. Using (
3.17
), for
(a,b)\neq(1,1)
, we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\eqref{eq:esti-wei-time-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\phi_{\theta_{1}}^{a}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\phi_{\theta_{2}}^{b}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}+2H(2){\delta}}.

If
(a,b)=(1,1)
, by Lemma
3.4
, (
3.17
), and (
3.26
), we then estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\eqref{eq:esti-wei-time-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}}2^{-\frac{3k}{2}}\left\|\phi_{\theta_{1}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\phi_{\theta_{2}}^{\leq J,k_{2}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{8}}.

The estimates for (
3.29
) can be treated similarly.

When the derivative falls on
|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in (
3.27
), an additional factor of
|\xi|^{-1}
appears. Though we are given this singular factor, since it corresponds to the growth
{\left\langle t\right\rangle}^{\frac{2}{3}+10H(2){\delta}}
from the assumption of this lemma, we can handle this case by using the similar methods to those detailed above.
∎

4.
Nonlinear estimates

In this section we consider nonlinear estimates for Dirac part and Maxwell part based on the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
) and linear estimates of Section
3
.

4.1.
Nonlinear estimates for Dirac part

Let us invoke the nonlinearity of Dirac part (
2.13
) and define the nonlinearity by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{N}}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}:=

\displaystyle\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)+\sum_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi).

Then

\displaystyle(-i\partial_{t}+\theta{\left\langle D\right\rangle})\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}={\mathfrak{N}}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}},\quad-i\partial_{t}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}=e^{\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}.

(4.1)

We will first prove the
L^{2}
boundedness of
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
. Let
t\in[0,T]
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, and
\theta\in\{+,-\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\mathfrak{N}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|P_{k}\partial_{t}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)-5}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{k}),

(4.2)

where

\displaystyle N(4)=0,\;\;\widetilde{H}(0):=5,\;\;\widetilde{H}(n):=H(n)+160.

Proof.

By the boundedness of
\Pi_{\theta}
,
R_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, and
e^{\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}
it suffices to show the following bilinear estimate

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)-5}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{k}),\end{aligned}

(4.3)

for
\mathcal{L}_{l}\in\mathcal{V}_{l},\,\theta_{l}\in\{+,-\}\,(l=1,2)
,
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n
, and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
By Hölder’s inequality and (
3.15
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}\|\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\right]{\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})},

where
\mathbf{k}:=(k,k_{1},k_{2})
. This yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\right]{\delta}}2^{k},

which implies (
4.2
) in the case that
2^{k}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
.

Let us consider the case
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
. From (
3.19
) and (
3.20
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n_{1}+1){\delta}}2^{\frac{k_{1}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1}+1)+2}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{2k_{1}}),\\
\|W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n_{2}+1){\delta}+\frac{{\delta}}{2}}2^{\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2}+1)+2}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{k_{2}}),\end{aligned}

(4.4)

for
0\leq n_{1},n_{2}\leq 2
. We partition the set of numbers of vector fields as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{n}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2}):n_{1}+n_{2}=n,0\leq n_{1},n_{2}\leq n\}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2}):n_{1}+n_{2}=n,n_{1},n_{2}\geq 1\}\cup\{(n,0),(0,n):n\geq 1\}\cup\{(0,0)\}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{N}_{n}^{1}\cup\mathcal{N}_{n}^{2}\cup\{(0,0)\}.

Estimates for
(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{1}
and
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
.
When
2^{k}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{k}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}}

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2})\right]{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

Note that
H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2})\leq H(n)+10
for
(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{1}
. Analogously, if
2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}}

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n)+11\right]{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})}.

Estimates for
(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{2}
and
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
.
If
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
, then we consider the cases
2^{k_{1}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
and
2^{k_{1}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
, for which we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{k_{1}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}}

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k_{1}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n)+2\right]{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{k_{1}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}}

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k_{1}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n)+12\right]{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

These finish the proof of (
4.3
) when
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
.

If
(n_{1},n_{2})=(0,n)
, we only consider the case
2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{1}}
since apart from this case, we can obtain the estimates similar to the above. By (
3.18
), we estimate

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{[H(1)+H(n)]{\delta}}2^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)-1}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

This enables us to get (
4.3
) if
2^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
.

To handle the case
2^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
, we make the decomposition
P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}=\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}+\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}
with
2^{J}=C2^{k_{1}}\left\langle t\right\rangle
for some
C\ll 1
. In view of (
3.26
) and (
3.17
), we see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|e^{-{\theta_{1}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-\frac{k_{1}}{2}+\frac{k_{1}}{1000}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-19},\\
\|\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+H(1){\delta}}2^{-k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}.\end{aligned}

(4.5)

Then we have

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{1}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|e^{-{\theta_{1}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}+H(n){\delta}}2^{-\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}+\frac{k_{1}}{2000}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-19}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}

and

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{1}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}(t)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}\|\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+\left[H(n)+H(1)\right]{\delta}}2^{k_{2}-k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

These give us the desired estimates in case
2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{1}}
and
2^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
.

Estimates for
(n_{1},n_{2})=(0,0)
.
By Hölder’s inequality, we estimate

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}\right)(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2{\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)},

which shows (
4.3
) if
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
or
\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{30}}
.

Let us now consider

\mbox{Case A: }2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}\mbox{ and }\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{30}}.

By Plancherel’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}{\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}\right)\|_{L^{2}}=\|\mathcal{F}\left[P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}{\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}\right)\right]\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}.

In particular, one gets

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[P_{k}\left(P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(t)\right)\right](\xi)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)e^{itp_{12}(\xi,\eta)}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(t,\xi-\eta)d\eta,

where the phase

\displaystyle p_{12}(\xi,\eta)={\theta_{1}}\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle+{\theta_{2}}|\eta|.

As we observed in (
2.7
), this phase interaction does not exhibit a space resonance. We exploit this non-resonance feature as follows: By the relation

\displaystyle e^{itp_{12}(\xi,\eta)}=-it^{-1}\frac{\nabla_{\eta}p_{12}(\xi,\eta)\cdot\nabla_{\eta}e^{itp_{12}(\xi,\eta)}}{|\nabla_{\eta}p_{12}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}},

we integrate by parts with respect to
\eta
to obtain the following integrals

\displaystyle t^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itp_{12}(\xi,\eta)}\nabla_{\eta}M_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(t,\xi-\eta)d\eta,

(4.6)

\displaystyle t^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itp_{12}(\xi,\eta)}M_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}(xV_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}})}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(t,\xi-\eta)d\eta,

(4.7)

\displaystyle t^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itp_{12}(\xi,\eta)}M_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}x\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(t,\xi-\eta)d\eta,

(4.8)

where

\displaystyle M_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{\nabla_{\eta}p_{12}(\xi,\eta)}{|\nabla_{\eta}p_{12}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\rho_{k}(\xi)\rho_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\rho_{k_{2}}(\eta).

Then, a direct calculation yields that

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\eta}M_{12,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim 2^{-k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{4}.

Using Hölder’s inequality and (
3.15
), we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm Case\,A}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|\eqref{eq:nonlinear-space-1}\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{\rm Case\,A}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+2{\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-3k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+4}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+3{\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(1)-5}.

This leads us (
4.3
). For (
4.7
), we have

\displaystyle\|M_{12,\mathbf{k}}\|_{{\rm CM}}\lesssim\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{8}.

Then, by Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
3.4
