, this implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm Case\,A}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|\eqref{eq:nonlinear-space-2}\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\rm Case\,A}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{2}\|P_{k_{2}}(xV_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}})(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{\theta_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\sum_{\rm Case\,A}2^{\frac{\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+[H(1)+H(0)]{\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm Case\,A}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|\eqref{eq:nonlinear-space-2}\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\rm Case\,A}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{8}\|P_{k_{2}}(xV_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}})(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{\theta_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\sum_{\rm Case\,A}2^{-k_{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-2+2H(1){\delta}+\frac{1}{2}{\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+10}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+8},

respectively. By decomposing
2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
2^{k_{2}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, we complete the bound for (
4.7
). The estimates for (
4.8
) can be obtained analogously.
∎

We will use the following lemma to prove weighted energy estimates and asymptotic behavior.

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
. Let Let
t\in[0,T]
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},0\leq n\leq 2
, and
l\in\{1,2,3\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\left(x_{l}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}\right)(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)-5}.

(4.9)

Proof.

Since
[x_{l},\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}]
is smoother than
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, it suffices to consider only the first term of
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}
as previously.
By Plancherel’s theorem, we see that

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)\partial_{\xi_{l}}\left[\widehat{W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}\widehat{\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi-\eta)\right]d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}.

(4.10)

The derivative
\partial_{\xi_{l}}
falls on either
e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}
or
\widehat{\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi-\eta)
. If the derivative falls on the phase modulation
e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}
, Lemma
4.1
leads us to the bound of (
4.9
). Thus, we have only to consider the case when the derivative falls on
\widehat{\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi-\eta)
.

By (
3.15
), (
3.16
), and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi-\eta)d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k_{1}}\left(x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}\right)(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2})\right]{\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1}+1)-1}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})},\end{aligned}

(4.11)

which implies the bound of (
4.9
) if
n_{1}\neq n
.

Let us consider the case
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
. Using (
4.11
), we get the bound of (
4.9
) if
2^{k_{1}}\ll 2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{2}}
and
2^{k_{1}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-10{\delta}}
. By (
3.17
) and (
3.20
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi-\eta)d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|P_{k_{1}}\left(x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}\right)(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n_{1}+1)+H(1)\right]{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1}+1)-1}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}.\end{aligned}

(4.12)

If
2^{k_{1}}\ll 2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{2}}
and
2^{k_{1}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-10{\delta}}
, then we get the desired bound by (
4.12
). In addition, we are done for the case
2^{k}\lesssim 2^{k_{1}}\sim 2^{k_{2}}
or the case
2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{1}}
and
2^{k}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{20}}
.

Now it remains to consider the case
2^{k_{2}}\ll 2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{1}}
and
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{20}}
. Making the change of variables in the integral of (
4.10
), for (
4.10
) we need to estimate

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)e^{{\theta_{2}}it|\xi-\eta|}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}x_{l}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\xi-\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\eta)d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}.

This leads us that, by Bernstein’s inequality,

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)e^{{\theta_{2}}it|\xi-\eta|}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}x_{l}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(t,\xi-\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\eta)d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\left(x_{l}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}\right)(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n)+H(1)\right]{\delta}}2^{k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}.

Here we used
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{20}}
to the bound
t^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}}
. This completes the proof of (
4.9
).
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
. Let
t\in[0,T]
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, and
\theta\in\{+,-\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{N}_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}+2H(3){\delta}}.

(4.13)

We note that Lemma
4.3
is improved in terms of the regularity condition, whereas decay effect has a loss.

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

For
\mathcal{L}_{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{1}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{2}}
(
0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}=n
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(t))=\sum_{{\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}\in\{\pm\}}A_{\mu,{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{1}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t).

Then it suffices to show

\displaystyle\|A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}+2H(3){\delta}}

for
\mathcal{L}_{l}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{l}}(l=1,2)
. To this end, we decompose
A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}=\sum_{k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{P_{k_{2}}}A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}
. If
2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}}
, Hölder inequality with (
3.15
) yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}}}2^{k_{2}}\|\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}+2H(3){\delta}}.

Let us consider
2^{k_{2}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}}
. If
n\leq 2
, using (
3.15
), (
3.19
), and (
3.20
), we see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|{P_{k_{2}}}A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}&\lesssim 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\qquad+2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}+2H(3){\delta}}.\end{aligned}

(4.14)

Next, we treat the case
n=3
. When
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
or
(n_{1},n_{2})=(0,n)
, we similarly have

\displaystyle\|{P_{k_{2}}}A_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\|\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{p}}\|\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}+2H(3){\delta}},

where
(p,q)=(\infty,2)
when
(n_{1},n_{2})=(0,n)
and
(p,q)=(2,\infty)
when
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
. Then it remains to handle the case
1\leq n_{1},n_{2}\leq n-1
when
n=3
. However, this case can be done by the same estimates to (
4.14
). Therefore, we complete the proof of (
4.13
).
∎

4.2.
Nonlinear estimates for Maxwell part

We denote the nonlinear term of Maxwell part (
2.16
) by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{N}}_{\mu,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}:=\frac{1}{2}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle.

Then for
\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}

\displaystyle(i\partial_{t}+\theta^{\prime}|D|)W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}=\theta^{\prime}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\mu,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}},\qquad i\partial_{t}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}=\theta^{\prime}e^{-i\theta^{\prime}t|D|}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\mu,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}.

(4.15)

Lemma 4.4
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
is a solution to (
1.2
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
and satisfies (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, and
\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\mu,\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|P_{k}\partial_{t}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n)+5}\min(2^{k^{-}},\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}).

(4.16)

Note that
\widetilde{H}(n)
is defined in Lemma
4.1
.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

We partition the range of
|\eta|
and
|\xi+\eta|
with dyadic numbers
2^{k_{1}}
and
2^{k_{2}}

(k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}})
, respectively. Then, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\right\rangle\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{H}(n)}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)+5}\min(\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},2^{k})\end{aligned}

(4.17)

for
\mathcal{L}_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{l}}\,(l=1,2)
and
n_{1}+n_{2}=n
. We also assume that
n_{1}\geq n_{2}
due to the symmetry between
\psi_{\theta_{1}}
and
\psi_{\theta_{2}}
. By (
3.15
), a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\right\rangle\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\left[H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\right]{\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})}.

Since
H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\leq\widetilde{H}(n)
and
\min(N(n_{1}),N(n_{2}))=N(n)
, we get (
4.17
) if
2^{k}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
.

Let us assume that
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
. In this case, we estimate by dividing the distribution of vector fields as follows:

\displaystyle(n_{1},n_{2})\in\{(2,1),(1,1)\}\cup\{(n,0):n\geq 1\}\cup\{(0,0)\}.

Estimates for
(n_{1},n_{2})\in\{(2,1),(1,1)\}
.
Analogously to the proof of Lemma
4.1
, we exploit the space resonance. Taking Fourier transform to integrand of left-hand side in (
4.17
), we see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{F}\left[P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\right\rangle\right](\xi)\\
&=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{itq_{12}(\xi,\eta)}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi+\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}}(t,\eta)\right\rangle d\eta,\end{aligned}

(4.18)

where

\displaystyle q_{12}(\xi,\eta)={\theta_{1}}\left\langle\xi+\eta\right\rangle-{\theta_{2}}\left\langle\eta\right\rangle.

As we observed in (
2.9
), we exploit the resonance cases according to
{\theta_{1}}={\theta_{2}}
and
{\theta_{1}}\neq{\theta_{2}}
.

Let us first consider the case
{\theta_{1}}={\theta_{2}}
. By performing the integration by parts in
\eta
, (
4.18
) is the sum of the following:

\displaystyle t^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{12}(\xi,\eta)}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{M}_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi+\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}}(t,\eta)\right\rangle d\eta,

(4.19)

\displaystyle t^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{12}(\xi,\eta)}\widetilde{M}_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{1}}x\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi+\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}}(t,\eta)\right\rangle d\eta,

(4.20)

\displaystyle t^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{itq_{12}(\xi,\eta)}\widetilde{M}_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi+\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{2}}x\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}}(t,\eta)\right\rangle d\eta,

(4.21)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)=|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\nabla_{\eta}q_{12}(\xi,\eta)}{|\nabla_{\eta}q_{12}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\rho_{\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta).

A direct calculation leads us that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{\eta}^{\ell}\widetilde{M}_{12,\mathbf{k}}(\xi,\eta)\right|\lesssim 2^{-\frac{3k}{2}}2^{-\ell\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\max\left(\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle,\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle\right)^{3+\ell}

for
\ell=0,1
. Then, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eq:nonlinear-max-space-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-3k}{2}}2^{-\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\max\left(\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle,\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle\right)^{4}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{[H(n_{1})+H(n_{2}+1)]{\delta}-1}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-3k}{2}-\min(k_{1},k_{2})+k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})+4}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2}+1)+4}.

The above estimate follows from (
3.15
) and (
3.18
) for
\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}
and
\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}
, respectively. Summing over
k_{1},k_{2}
, we obtain the bound for (
4.19
).

By (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eq:nonlinear-max-space-2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-3k}{2}}\max\left(\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle,\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle\right)^{3}\|P_{k_{1}}(x\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}})(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{[H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2})]{\delta}-1}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-3k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1}+1)+3}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})+3}.

Since
H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2})=H(n)+10
and
N(n)-5\leq N(n_{1}+1)-3
, this implies (
4.17
). Similarly, the estimate of (
4.21
) can be carried out and hence we omit the details.

We now turn to the case
{\theta_{1}}\neq{\theta_{2}}
. In view of (
2.9
), the phase interaction
q_{12}(\xi,\eta)
exhibits the space resonance when
\xi+2\eta=0
. If
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\ll 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}
, then by the frequency relation, we have
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\sim|\xi+2\eta|
. Using the integration by parts in
\eta
, this case can be treated in a similar way to the case
{\theta_{1}}={\theta_{2}}
. Thus, we have only to consider

2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\sim 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}.

The estimate for this case is straightforward. Indeed, by (
3.15
) and (
3.19
), we see that

\displaystyle\|\eqref{eq:bilinear-fourier}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{[H(n_{1})+H(n_{2}+1)]{\delta}-1}2^{\frac{-k+k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2}+1)+2}.

This finishes the proof of (
4.17
) when
(n_{1},n_{2})\in\{(2,1),(1,1)\}
.

Estimates for
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
and
n\geq 1
.
From (
3.15
) and (
3.5
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}}}(t)\right\rangle\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n){\delta}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}2^{(1+5H(2){\delta})k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+2}.

If
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+130{\delta}}
or
2^{k_{2}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{40}}
, this estimate implies (
4.17
). If
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}\gg\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+130{\delta}}
and
2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{40}}
, we decompose
P_{k_{2}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}}}
into
\phi^{\leq J,k_{2}}_{{\theta_{2}}}
and
\phi^{>J,k_{2}}_{{\theta_{2}}}
with
J=C2^{k_{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle
for some
C\ll 1
. And we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|e^{-\theta it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi^{\leq J,k_{2}}_{{\theta_{2}}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}+\frac{k_{2}}{2000}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-19},\\
\left\|\phi^{>J,k_{2}}_{{\theta_{2}}}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+H(1){\delta}}2^{-k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)},\end{aligned}

(4.22)

which follow from (
3.26
) and (
3.17
), respectively. On the one hand,
L^{2}\times L^{\infty}
estimate leads us to the bound

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{2}}it\left\langle D\right\rangle}\phi_{{\theta_{2}}}^{\leq J,k}(t)\right\rangle\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n){\delta}-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-\frac{k+k_{2}}{2}+\frac{k_{2}}{1000}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-19}.\end{aligned}

(4.23)

If
2^{k_{2}}\lesssim 2^{k_{1}}
, (
4.23
) yields (
4.17
) over
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}\gg\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+130{\delta}}
and
2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{40}}
. If
2^{k_{1}}\ll 2^{k_{2}}
, then in view of (
4.17
) with
n=1
, we can observe that the factor
\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-19}
is not sufficient for
\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+5}
when
2^{k}\geq 1
. However, from the frequency restriction
2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{40}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-19}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{3}{20}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+5},

which enables to obtain the desired bound. On the other hand, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}e^{-{\theta_{2}}it\left\langle D\right\rangle}\phi_{{\theta_{2}}}^{>J,k}(t)\right\rangle\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{[H(n)+H(1)]{\delta}-1}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}-\frac{k}{2}-k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}.\end{aligned}

Them, (
4.17
) follows from the sum over
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}\gg\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+130{\delta}}
and
2^{k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{40}}
.

Estimates for
(n_{1},n_{2})=(0,0)
.
By the symmetry between
\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}
and
\psi_{{\theta_{2}}}
, we only consider the case
k_{1}\leq k_{2}
. If
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2{\delta}}
, using (
3.15
) and (
3.19
), we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}\leq k_{2}}\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}}}(t)\right\rangle\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\leq k_{2}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{\theta_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\psi_{\theta_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}\leq k_{2}}2^{\frac{-k+k_{1}}{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{11{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{{\delta}-1}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+5}.

If
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+5{\delta}}
, (
3.5
) yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}\leq k_{2}}\|P_{k}|D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(t),{\alpha}_{\mu}P_{k_{2}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}}}(t)\right\rangle\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\leq k_{2}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{\theta_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\psi_{\theta_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}\leq k_{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{{\delta}}2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{6{\delta}-1}.

Let us consider the mid frequency case
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+5{\delta}}
and
2^{k}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2{\delta}}
. Setting
J:=C2^{k_{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle
for some
C\ll 1
, we decompose
P_{k_{2}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}}}
into
\phi_{{\theta_{2}}}^{\leq J,k_{2}}
and
\phi_{\theta_{2}}^{>J,k_{2}}
. Then we obtain (
4.22
) and the remaining estimates can be done similarly to those for the case
(n_{1},n_{2})=(n,0)
and
n\geq 1
.
∎

The following lemma will be used in the proof of weighted estimates and scattering for the Maxwell part.

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>1
. Let
t\in[0,T]
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},0\leq n\leq 2
, and
l\in\{1,2,3\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{k}\left(x_{l}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}\right)(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)+5}.

(4.24)

Proof.

As described in the proof of Lemma
4.2
, one may consider only

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\rho_{k}(\xi)\left\langle e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle\xi+\eta\right\rangle}\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(t,\xi+\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}}(t,\eta)\right\rangle d\eta\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{2}}.

(4.25)

Note that
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
due to the symmetry. By (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), we see that

\displaystyle\|{P_{k_{1}}}(x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}})(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n_{1}+1){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1}+1)},

\displaystyle\|{P_{k_{2}}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n_{2}){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})}.

Hölder’s inequality yields that

\displaystyle\left|\eqref{eq:aim-nonlinear-wei-maxwell}\right|\lesssim\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}(x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}})(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}.

(4.26)

Since
H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2})\leq\widetilde{H}(n)
for
n=1,2
, we obtain (
4.24
) apart from the case
n=0
. Let us consider the case
n=0
. This case corresponds to the case
(n_{1},n_{2})=(0,0)
. Then, by the symmetry, it suffices to handle
2^{k_{1}}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}
. If
2^{k}\leq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
, we simply have (
4.24
) due to (
4.26
), If
2^{k}\geq\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1}
, using (
3.19
), we estimate

\displaystyle\left|\eqref{eq:aim-nonlinear-wei-maxwell}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k_{1}}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}(x_{l}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}})(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\psi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{2^{k_{1}}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}}{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+2H(1){\delta}}2^{\frac{-k+k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}.

This completes the proof of (
4.24
).
∎

5.
Proof of main theorem

For any initial data (
1.3
) satisfying (
1.5
), one can readily show the existence of local solution
(\psi,A_{\mu})
to (
2.1
) with
(\psi,W_{\mu})\in C([0,T];H^{N(0)}\cap C^{1}([0,T];H^{N(0)-1})
for some
T>0
by the regularity persistence based on the local theory of
[
8
]
. Then the standard approximation with
e^{-\lambda|x|^{2}}\left\langle x\right\rangle^{N}
is applicable to the system (
2.1
) and enables us to get, for sufficiently small
T
,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sum_{n=1,2,3}\left[\|\left\langle x\right\rangle^{n}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{n}(\psi(t),W_{\mu}(t))\|_{H^{N(n)}}\right]\leq C(T,\psi(0),W_{\mu}(0)).

Now we show
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\psi}(t,\xi)\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}<+\infty
.
In fact, by (
2.2
) we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\widehat{\phi_{\theta}}(t)|\leq|\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\widehat{\psi_{\theta}}(0)|+c\sum_{{\theta_{1}}\in\{\pm\}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\widehat{W_{\mu}}(s,\eta)||\widehat{\phi_{\theta_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)|\,d\eta ds.

Let
f(t)=\|\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\psi_{\theta}}(t)\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}
. Then using

\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle^{20}|\xi-\eta|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle^{20}|\eta|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left\langle\eta\right\rangle^{20}|\xi-\eta|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left\langle\eta\right\rangle^{20}|\eta|^{\frac{1}{2}},

the factor
|\eta|^{\frac{1}{2}}
gets rid of the singularity of
|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and Hölder’s inequality leads us to the bound

f(t)\leq C_{T}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|W_{\mu}(s)\|_{H^{N(0)}}f(s)\,ds\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;C_{T}=CT\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|(\psi(t),W_{\mu}(t))\|_{H^{N(0)}}^{2}.

Hence Gronwall’s inequality shows
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}f(t)\leq C_{T}
.
If
T
is sufficiently small, then one can readily verify the local solution satisfies the a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
) via (
2.15
) and (
2.17
).

Remark 5.1
.

The above local properties are not good enough for the global extension due to the bad growth in time. One may take bootstrap argument based on the weighted energy
\||x|^{2}(\phi_{\theta},V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}})\|_{H^{m}}
as in
[
6
]
. But the square weight makes a trouble because it plays a role as the second derivative in the Fourier side and gives rise to a serious singularity to the nonlinearity. This obstacle prevents us from closing bootstrap due to the lack of null structure like (
1.12
). This is a reason why we use bootstrap argument based on the vector-field energy method.

Now we extend the solution globally by time continuity. To this end, we go through several steps of bootstrap argument, which are energy, weighted energy, and scattering norm estimates to be implemented under a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Each step is described as the proposition below in which solution turns out to have more improvement than a priori assumptions. Since the proof of propositions when
T\lesssim 1
is similar to (much simpler than) the case
T\gg 1
, we assume that
T\gg 1
from now. Let us introduce propositions running bootstrap.

Proposition 5.2

(Energy estimates)
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
, and
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{H^{N(n)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n){\delta}},

(5.1)

\displaystyle\left\|W_{\mu,{\mathcal{L}},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{H^{N(n)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n){\delta}}.

(5.2)

Proposition 5.3

(Weighted energy estimates)
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
n\in\{0,1,2\}
,
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
,
l\in\{1,2,3\}
, and
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle\|P_{k}\left(x_{l}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}\right)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n+1){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)},

(5.3)

\displaystyle 2^{k}\|P_{k}\left(x_{l}V_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}\right)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{H(n+1){\delta}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)}.

(5.4)

Proposition 5.4

(Scattering norm estimates)
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\{+,-\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\|_{\mathbf{D}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2},

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t)\|_{\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}.

(5.6)

The proof of propositions will be given in Section
6
, Section
7
, and Sections
8
and
9
, respectively.

Proof of the asymptotic behavior for the Maxwell-Dirac system.
The above propositions close the bootstrap argument and lead us the global existence immediately. Now we show the asymptotic behavior parts of the main theorem. Let us first consider the modified scattering for Dirac part. In Section
8
we will show that for
0<t_{1}\leq t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{P_{k}\Psi_{\theta}}(t_{2},\xi)-\widehat{P_{k}\Psi_{\theta}}(t_{1},\xi)\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t_{2}\right\rangle^{-{\delta}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}+\frac{1}{100}k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-20},

where
\Psi_{\theta}(t,\xi)=e^{-iB_{\theta}(t,\xi)}\widehat{\phi_{\theta}}(t,\xi)
and
B_{\theta}(t,\xi)
is the phase modification symbol as defined in Theorem
1.1
.
This bound shows the existence of limit
\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\phi_{\theta}(t^{\prime})
in
L^{2}
.
By setting

\psi_{\theta}^{\infty}(t):=e^{-\theta it\left\langle D\right\rangle}\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\phi_{\theta}(t^{\prime}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\|\psi_{\theta}(t)-e^{iB_{\theta}(t,D)}\psi_{\theta}^{\infty}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{20}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{F}\left[\psi_{\theta}(t)-e^{iB_{\theta}(t,D)}\psi_{\theta}^{\infty}(t)\right]\right\|_{L_{\xi}^{\infty}}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-{\delta}}{\varepsilon}_{0}.

Note that
\psi^{\infty}:=\Pi_{+}\psi_{+}^{\infty}+\Pi_{-}\psi_{-}^{\infty}
is a solution to linear Dirac equation.

On the other hand, for the proof of (
5.6
), we will show that for
0<t_{1}\leq t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}2^{j}\left\|Q_{jk}V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t_{2})-Q_{jk}V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t_{2}\right\rangle^{-{\delta}}2^{-k-5H(2){\delta}k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+5}.

Then this implies the existence of limit
\lim A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}
in
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+5H(2){\delta}}
.
By setting

A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}^{\infty}(t):=e^{\theta^{\prime}it|D|}\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t^{\prime}),

we see that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle D\right\rangle^{25}\left(A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t)-A_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}^{\infty}(t)\right)\right\|_{{\dot{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}+5H(2){\delta}}}\lesssim\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-{\delta}}{\varepsilon}_{0}.

Utilizing (
3.19
) and (
3.20
), one can readily establish (
1.9
) with
\overline{\delta}:=11\delta
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

6.
Proof of energy estimates

This section is devoted to proving Proposition
5.2
.

6.1.
Proof of (
5.1
): the bound on
\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}

We first prove the energy estimates for the Dirac part.
In order to show (
5.1
) let us define the energy functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t),\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\rangle-\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t),A_{\mu}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\rangle dx,

where
\mathcal{P}^{D}:=\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{P}^{D^{\prime}}:=\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. Then, we have
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}\sim\|\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}\|_{H^{N(n)}}^{2}
since
{\varepsilon}_{1}\ll 1
. Using (
2.13
), we see that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}\psi_{\theta}=-\theta i{\left\langle D\right\rangle}\mathcal{L}\psi_{\theta}+i\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)+i\theta\sum_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi).

(6.1)

Then, this yields that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{D}}(t)&=2{\rm Im}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left[\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta},\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{P}^{D}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)\right\rangle+\theta\sum_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta},\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}{\left\langle D\right\rangle}^{N(n)}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)\right\rangle\right]\,dx\\
&\qquad-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t),\end{aligned}

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)=

\displaystyle 2{\rm Im}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t),A_{\mu}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\rangle dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t),\partial_{t}A_{\mu}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\rangle dx.

The following proposition finishes the energy estimates for Dirac part.

Proposition 6.1
.

Assume that
(\psi,A_{\mu})
satisfies a priori assumptions (
3.3
)–(
3.5
). Let
t\in[0,T]
,
n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
, and
\theta\in\{+,-\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left|2{\rm Im}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t),\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{P}^{D}(A_{\mu}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(t))\right\rangle dx-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(s)ds\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2H(n){\delta}},

(6.2)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s),\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}{\left\langle D\right\rangle}^{N(n)}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(s))\right\rangle dxds\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2H(n){\delta}}.

(6.3)

Proof.

Let us first consider (
6.2
). Regarding
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(s)
, by (
6.1
) we see that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(s)

\displaystyle=2{\rm Im}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s),A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s)\right\rangle dx

\displaystyle\quad-2{\rm Re}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{P}^{D}(A_{\nu}(s){\alpha}^{\nu}\psi(s)),A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s)\right\rangle dx

\displaystyle\quad-\theta 2{\rm Re}\sum_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n^{\prime}}}C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(A_{\nu}(s){\alpha}^{\nu}\psi(s)),A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s)\right\rangle dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t),\partial_{t}A_{\mu}(t){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(t)\right\rangle dx

\displaystyle=:\sum_{j=1}^{4}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{j}(s).

Using Lemma
4.3
and (
3.20
), we estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(s)+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}(s)ds\right|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2H(n){\delta}}.

Moreover, by (
3.25
) we obtain that for
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}A_{\mu}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\theta^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\|\partial_{t}P_{k}W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1},

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{4}(s)ds\right|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2H(n){\delta}}.

Thus, it suffices to consider the part

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s),\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{P}^{D}(A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(s))\right\rangle\,dx-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(s)ds,

which can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left\langle\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi_{\theta}(s),\Pi_{\theta}\mathcal{P}^{D}(A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(s))-\Pi_{\theta}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\left\langle D\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{D}\psi(s)\right)\right\rangle\,dxds.

(6.4)

By commutation relation one gets

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)=\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}\psi)+\sum_{\mathcal{L}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{\ell}},\ell=1,2}C(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}(\mathcal{L}_{2}A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{1}\psi),

where
C(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})
are constants depending only on
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}
and
n_{1}\leq n-1,n_{1}+n_{2}=n
.

We can write (
6.4
) in the Fourier side as the sum of the followig:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(2\pi)^{-6}\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,\\
&(2\pi)^{-6}\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,\end{aligned}

(6.5)

where

\displaystyle M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle:=|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{N(n)+\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{N(n)-\frac{1}{2}}-\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle^{N(n)-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle:=|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Pi_{\theta}(\xi)\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}.

We estimate (
6.5
) by dividing the time
t\in[0,T]
into a dyadic pieces
2^{m}

(m\in\{0,\cdots,L+1\})
with

|L-\log_{2}(t+2)|\leq 2

and by taking cut-offs
q_{m}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle{\rm supp}(q_{0})\subset[0,2],\quad{\rm supp}(q_{L+1})\subset[t-2,t],\quad{\rm supp}(q_{m})\subset[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}],

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{m=0}^{L+1}q_{m}(s)=\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}(s),\quad q_{m}\in C^{1}({\mathbb{R}}),\qquad\int_{0}^{t}|q_{m}^{\prime}(s)|ds\lesssim 1.

Let
I_{m}
denote the support of
q_{m}
and let

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{m}^{1}:=\int_{I_{m}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}q_{m}(s)M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle\,d\xi d\eta ds,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{m}^{2}:=\int_{I_{m}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}q_{m}(s)M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle\,d\xi d\eta ds.

To get the bound (
6.2
), it suffices to show that for each
l=1,2
and
m\in\{0,\cdots,L+1\}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{m}^{l}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.

(6.6)

Estimates for
\mathcal{I}_{m}^{1}
.
To this end, we divide the frequencies of
\mathcal{I}_{m}^{1}
into the dyadic pieces
2^{k},2^{k_{1}},2^{k_{2}}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{m,\mathbf{k}}^{1}:=\int_{I_{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}q_{m}(s)M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

where
\mathbf{k}=(k,k_{1},k_{2})
. For the multiplier estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\|M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)\rho_{k}(\xi)\rho_{k_{1}}(\xi+\eta)\rho_{k_{2}}(\eta)\right\|_{{\rm CM}}\lesssim 2^{\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}.

Thus by Lemma
3.4
, (
3.15
), and (
3.25
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{m,\mathbf{k}}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{I_{m}}2^{\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\|P_{k}\psi_{{\theta},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{2H(n){\delta}m-m}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}2^{k_{2}-5H(2){\delta}k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-22},

which implies (
6.6
) in case that
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{2}
.

Let us move on to the case
\min(\mathbf{k})=k
or
k_{1}
. If
\min(\mathbf{k})\leq-\frac{2}{3}m-{\delta}m
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left|\mathcal{I}_{m,\mathbf{k}}^{1}\right|\\
&\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}+\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\|P_{k}\psi_{{\theta},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[2H(n)+1]{\delta}m}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1-N(0)}.\end{aligned}

(6.7)

We consider the high frequency case
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{\frac{1}{60}m}
. In this case the high frequency sum can be easily treated by the regularity condition
\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)}
of
W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}
in the energy estimates (
3.15
). To exploit this regularity gap, we estimate to distinguish
n\leq 2
and
n=3
. Indeed,
if
n\leq 2
, it follows from (
3.19
) that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{m,\mathbf{k}}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\|P_{k}\psi_{{\theta},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[H(n)+H(n+1)+1]{\delta}m-m}2^{\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)+3}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1-N(0)}.

On the other hand, if
n=3
, (
6.7
) yields that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{m,\mathbf{k}}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[2H(3)+1]{\delta}m}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})+k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(3)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(3)-1-N(0)}.

whose
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{\frac{m}{60}}
leads us to (
6.6
).

Let us now consider
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-\frac{2}{3}m-{\delta}m}
and
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{60}}
. For this, we use the normal form approach. Making integration by parts in time,
\mathcal{I}_{m,\mathbf{k}}^{1}
becomes the sum of the integrals:

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\!q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\frac{e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}}{p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi-\eta),\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.8)

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\!\!q_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\frac{e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}}{p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}M_{n}^{1}(\xi,\eta)\partial_{s}\left[\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi-\eta),\right\rangle\right]d\eta d\xi ds,

(6.9)

where
p_{\Theta}
is the phase defined in (
2.4
). By (
3.15
) and (
3.25
) we have

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:normal-dirac1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-m}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\|\partial_{s}P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{2H(n){\delta}m-2m}2^{-5H(2){\delta}k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-23}.

This enables us to sum over
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-\frac{2}{3}m-{\delta}m}
and
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{60}}
. To estimate (
6.9
), we utilize

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|P_{k}\partial_{s}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{-m+\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)-5},\\
\left\|P_{k}\partial_{s}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{-m+\widetilde{H}(0){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(1)-5},\end{aligned}

(6.10)

obtained by (
4.2
) and (
4.16
), respectively. In (
6.9
), if the time derivative falls on
\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}
or
\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}}
, we estimate

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:normal-dirac2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\|\partial_{s}P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[H(n)+\widetilde{H}(n)]{\delta}m-2m}2^{-5H(2){\delta}k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)-N(n+1)-5}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-23}.

We consider the case that the derivative falls on
V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}
. In this case, we decompose
n\leq 2
and
n=3
. If
n\leq 2
, by (
3.19
) and (
6.10
) we obtain

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:normal-dirac2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-1}\|P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{H^{N(n)}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}{P_{k_{2}}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[H(n)+H(n+1)+\widetilde{H}(0)]{\delta}m-2m}2^{\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)+2}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)-6-N(1)}.

Let us move on to the case
n=3
. Using Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality and (
3.25
), we see that

\displaystyle\|e^{{\theta_{2}}it|D|}\partial_{s}{P_{k_{2}}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\||D|^{-\frac{1}{2}}{P_{k_{2}}}\left\langle\psi(s),{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi(s)\right\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\theta_{3},\theta_{4}\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}\in\mathcal{U}_{k_{\ell}}\\
k_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ell=3,4\end{subarray}}\|Q_{j_{3},k_{3}}\psi_{\theta_{3}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{3}}\|Q_{j_{3},k_{3}}\psi_{\theta_{3}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2}{3}}\|Q_{j_{4},k_{4}}\psi_{{\theta_{3}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}2^{-\frac{5}{3}m+20{\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+3}.

Then we have

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:normal-dirac2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(3)-1}\|P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{H^{N(3)}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{{\theta_{2}}it|D|}\partial_{s}{P_{k_{2}}}V_{\mu,{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[2H(3)+20]{\delta}m-\frac{5}{3}m}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(3)}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(3)-N(1)+2}.

This finishes the proof for (
6.9
).
Summing over
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-\frac{2}{3}m-{\delta}m}
and
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{60}}
, we get (
6.6
).

Remark 6.2
.

Regarding the restriction
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{60}}
, we need this condition only when
n=0,1
and
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{1}
in the estimates for (
6.8
). In view of (
6.10
), we also require this restriction in the estimates for (
6.9
) when
n\leq 2
.

Estimates for
\mathcal{I}_{m}^{2}
.
We postpone a proof for the case
n_{1}=0
to Section
6.3
. Here, we consider the cases
(n_{1},n_{2})=(2,1)
, and
(1,2)
when
n=3
and
(n_{1},n_{2})=(1,1)
when
n=2
. Similarly to the previous estimates, we separate
|\xi|,|\xi+\eta|,|\eta|
by dyadic numbers
2^{k},2^{k_{1}},2^{k_{2}}
and consider the integral

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}q_{m}(s)M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds.

(6.11)

If
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{-m}
or
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{10}}
, by Hölder’s inequality and (
3.15
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\eqref{eq:e-3-dec-2}|&\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)}\|P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{\left(H(n)+H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\right){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})}.\end{aligned}

Since
1\leq n_{1},n_{2}\leq n-1
, one gets
H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\leq H(n)-190
and
N(n)\leq\min\left(N(n_{1}),N(n_{2})\right)-10
. Summing over
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{-m}
or
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{10}}
, one can get (
6.6
).

If
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
and
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{10}}
, then we exploit the space resonance (
2.7
). More precisely, making integration by parts in
\eta
, (
6.11
) becomes the sum of the following:

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}s^{-1}q_{m}(s)e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{M_{n,\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.12)

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}s^{-1}q_{m}(s)e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\widetilde{M_{n,\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}xV_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.13)

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}s^{-1}q_{m}(s)e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\widetilde{M_{n,\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}x\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.14)

where the multiplier
\widetilde{M_{n}^{2}}
is defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{M_{n,\mathbf{k}}^{2}}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{\nabla_{\eta}p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)}{|\nabla_{\eta}p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\rho_{k}(\xi)\rho_{k_{1}}(\xi+\eta)\rho_{k_{2}}(\eta).

In view of (
2.7
), this multiplier satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{\eta}^{\ell}\widetilde{M_{n,\mathbf{k}}^{2}}(\xi,\eta)\right|\lesssim 2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}-\ell k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{2+2\ell}

for
\ell=0,1
. Then, (
3.15
) leads us to the bound

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:e-space-1}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{\left(H(n)+H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\right){\delta}m}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}-\frac{3k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})+4}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})}.

The sum of (
6.12
) over
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
now gives us (
6.6
).

As for (
6.13
), by using (
3.16
) and (
3.19
), we estimate as follows:

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:e-space-2}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{\left(H(n)+H(n_{1})+H(n_{2}+1)\right){\delta}m}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})}{2}-k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2}+1)}

(6.15)

and

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:e-space-2}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{\left(H(n)+H(n_{1}+1)+H(n_{2}+1)\right){\delta}m}2^{-m}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1}+1)+8}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2}+1)}.

(6.16)

Since
H(n_{1})+H(n_{2}+1)\leq H(n)+10
and

\displaystyle 2^{-k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2}+1)}\lesssim\left\langle 2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{-10}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{-1},

one gets (
6.6
) by summing (
6.15
) over
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{10{\delta}m}
and (
6.16
) over
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{10{\delta}m}
.
The estimates for (
6.14
) can be obtained similarly with
L^{2}\times L^{2}\times L^{\infty}
estimates due to the restriction
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{\frac{m}{10}}
.

We prove (
6.3
). By (
2.14
) we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{n^{\prime}}}C(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\partial_{t}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)=C_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{0})\mathcal{L}_{0}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi)+\sum_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{\overline{n}}}C_{2}(\overline{\mathcal{L}})\mathcal{R}_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}\partial_{t}(A_{\mu}{\alpha}^{\mu}\psi),

for
\overline{n},\widetilde{n}\leq n-1
. By (
4.1
) and (
4.15
), it suffices to consider

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{3}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.17)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{4}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{W_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2},{\theta_{2}}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\mathfrak{N}^{\mathbf{D}}_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{4}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{\mathfrak{N}_{\mu,\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{\mathbf{M}}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,

(6.19)

where

\displaystyle M_{n}^{3}(\xi,\eta)=|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\mathcal{R}_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}}(\xi)\right)\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}(1+|\eta|+\left\langle\xi-\eta\right\rangle)\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;M_{n}^{4}(\xi,\eta)=|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{R}_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}}(\xi)\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}.

Since
R_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}}
is smooth and plays a similar role to
\Pi_{\theta}
, the proof of (
6.17
) can be done similarly to the estimates for
\mathcal{I}_{m}^{2}
by replacing
\Pi_{\theta},\mathcal{L}
with
\mathcal{R}_{\overline{\mathcal{L}}},\overline{\mathcal{L}}
, respectively. Indeed, the estimates for (
6.3
) can be obtained more easily from the fact that
n_{0},\overline{n}\leq n-1
. For the proofs of (
6.18
) and (
6.19
), Lemmas
4.1
and
4.4
imply the desired bounds straightforwardly. Therefore, we omit the proof of (
6.3
). ∎

6.2.
Proof of (
5.2
): the bound on
W_{\mu,{\mathcal{L}},\theta^{\prime}}

With
\mathcal{P}^{M}:=\left\langle D\right\rangle^{N(n)}|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{L}}
, we define the energy functional for Maxwell part

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}(t):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left[(\mathcal{P}^{M}A_{\mu}(t))^{2}+(\partial_{t}|D|^{-1}\mathcal{P}^{M}A_{\mu}(t))^{2}\right]dx.

(6.20)

Then, by the definition of
W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}(t)\sim\|W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(t)\|_{H^{N(n)}}^{2}.

Using the commutation relation of wave operator, we see that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{M}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|D|^{-1}\mathcal{P}^{M}[\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle](t)\partial_{t}|D|^{-1}\mathcal{P}^{M}A_{\mu}(t)dx

\displaystyle=i(2\pi)^{-3}\sum_{\theta^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}}\theta^{\prime}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}\mathcal{F}[{\mathcal{L}}\left\langle\psi,{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi\right\rangle](t,\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}\left[W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}\right]}(t,\xi)d\xi.

To prove (
6.20
), it suffices to show

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}q_{m}(s)|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}\mathcal{F}\left\langle\psi_{\theta_{1},\mathcal{L}_{1}},{\alpha}_{\mu}\psi_{\theta_{2},\mathcal{L}_{2}}\right\rangle(t,\xi)\overline{\widehat{W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}}}(t,\xi)d\xi ds\right|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m},

for any
t\in[0,T],m\in\{0,\cdots,L+1\},{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{1}},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{2}}(n_{1}+n_{2}=n)
, and
\theta^{\prime},\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\{+,-\}
. Then, we focus on the bound

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left|\int_{I_{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}q_{m}(s)|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}\left\langle\widehat{\psi_{\theta_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}}(s,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{\psi_{\theta_{2},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}}}(s,\xi)d\eta d\xi ds\right|\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.\end{aligned}

(6.21)

By the frequency decomposition, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mu,m,\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}(s)M(\xi)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\psi_{\theta_{2},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}}(s,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\psi_{\theta_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{P_{k}W_{\mu,{\mathcal{L}},\theta^{\prime}}}}(s,\xi)\,d\eta d\xi ds,

where
M(\xi)=|\xi|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}
and
\mathbf{k}=(k,k_{1},k_{2})
. To prove (
6.21
), it suffices to handle

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}|\mathcal{J}_{\mu,m,\,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}

(6.22)

for
n_{1},n_{2}\leq n\leq 3
,
\mathcal{L}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{V}_{n_{\ell}}
. To observe the resonances, we recall the phase interaction:

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M(\xi)e^{isq_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\phi_{\theta_{2},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}}(s,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{\theta_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{P_{k}V_{\mu,{\mathcal{L}},\theta^{\prime}}}(s,\xi)}d\eta d\xi ds,

where
q_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)
is as in (
2.5
).

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
. The proof of (
6.22
) is divided into the cases
n_{1}=0
and
n_{1}\geq 1
. The case
n_{1}=0(n\geq 1)
will be treated in Section
6.3
. Let us first consider the vector field free case
n=0
. From the symmetry between spinors, we assume that
k_{1}\leq k_{2}
. Then the bounds (
3.15
) and (
3.24
) yield that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\mu,m,\mathbf{k}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(0)}\|P_{k}W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(s)\|_{H^{N(0)}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{2H(0){\delta}m}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k}{2}+(1+\frac{1}{100})k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(0)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)}.

This implies (
6.22
) for
2^{k+k_{1}}\leq 2^{-m}
. Let us consider
2^{k+k_{1}}\geq 2^{-m}
. When
2^{k}\sim 2^{k_{2}}
, the estimate above yields the desired bound. Thus, we handle the case
2^{k}\ll 2^{k_{1}}\sim 2^{k_{2}}
. Using the decomposition and their bounds (
4.5
), we see that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\mu,m,\mathbf{k}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(0)}\|P_{k}W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(s)\|_{H^{N(0)}}\|e^{-{\theta_{1}}it\left\langle D\right\rangle}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{H(0){\delta}m-\frac{3}{2}m}2^{-\frac{k+k_{1}}{2}+\frac{k_{1}}{1000}+(1+\frac{1}{100})k_{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(0)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-19}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+2}

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\mu,m,\mathbf{k}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(0)}\|P_{k}W_{\mu,\theta^{\prime}}(s)\|_{H^{N(0)}}\|\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[H(0)+2H(1)]{\delta}m-2m}2^{-\frac{k}{2}-k_{1}+\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(0)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}.

These estimates give us (
6.22
).

Here we focus on the case
n_{1}\geq 1
, in which at least one vector field falls on each spinor. We first consider the cases
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{-m}
and
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
. When
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{-m}
. By (
3.15
) we obtain the bounds

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|P_{k}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta^{\prime}}(s)\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{H(n){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n)},\\
\left\|P_{k_{1}}\phi_{\theta_{1},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(s)\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{H(n_{1}){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})},\\
\left\|P_{k_{2}}\phi_{\theta_{2},\mathcal{L}_{2}}(s)\right\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{H(n_{2}){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})}.\end{aligned}

(6.23)

By Hölder’s inequality and (
6.23
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)}|\mathcal{J}_{m,\,\mathbf{k}}|\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{[H(n)+H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})]{\delta}m}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}2^{\frac{3}{2}\min(\mathbf{k})}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{1})}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n_{2})}.\end{aligned}

(6.24)

Since
H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})=H(n)-190
and
N(n)\leq N(n_{2})-10
, this directly implies (
6.22
). Let us now handle the case
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
. By integration by parts in time,
\mathcal{J}_{\mu,m,\mathbf{k}}
is written as the sum of the following

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\!q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\frac{e^{isq_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)}}{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\phi_{\theta_{2},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}}(s,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{\theta_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{P_{k}V_{\mu,{\mathcal{L}},\theta^{\prime}}}}(s,\xi)d\eta d\xi ds,

(6.25)

\displaystyle\int_{I_{m}}\!\!q_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\frac{e^{isq_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)}}{q_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta)}\partial_{s}\left[\left\langle\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\phi_{\theta_{2},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}}(s,\eta),{\alpha}_{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{\theta_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{P_{k}V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime},{\mathcal{L}}}}}(s,\xi)\right]d\eta d\xi ds.

(6.26)

Using Hölder’s inequality, we see that

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:normal-maxwell1}|\lesssim 2^{\frac{3}{2}\min(\mathbf{k})-k}\left\langle 2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\right\rangle^{2}\|P_{k}V_{\mu,\theta^{\prime},\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}.

Analogously to (
6.24
), we estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)}|\eqref{eq:normal-maxwell1}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.

Similar estimate to the above can be obtained for (
6.26
) by (
6.10
). Since
n_{1}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(n)+H(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\leq 2H(n)-30,

\displaystyle H(n)+\widetilde{H}(n_{1})+H(n_{2})\leq 2H(n)-30,

\displaystyle H(n)+H(n_{1})+\widetilde{H}(n_{2})\leq 2H(n)-30.

From this it follows that

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)}|\eqref{eq:normal-maxwell2}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m-30{\delta}m}\max\left(\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-1},\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-1}\right).

This completes the proof of (
6.22
) for
n_{1}\geq 1
.

6.3.
Bulk estimate on Dirac part

In this section, we finish the proof of the energy estimates of Dirac part for the cases that all vector fields fall on only one of the profiles. Let us invoke (
6.11
) with
n_{2}=n(n\geq 1)
and write

\mathcal{K}_{m,\mathbf{k}}:=\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}(s)\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}},}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds.

Note that
M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)=|\eta|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2N(n)}
. The following proposition gives us the proof of energy estimate on Dirac part.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume that
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
for
1\leq n\leq 3
and
t\in[0,T]
. Let
\mathbf{k}=(k_{0},k_{1},k_{2})
and
m\in\{0,\cdots,L+1\}
. Then
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}|\mathcal{K}_{m,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.

(6.27)

Proof.

We prove this proposition by decomposing the case into four cases.

Case 1:
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{-m}
.
We begin with
L^{\infty}\times L^{2}\times L^{2}
estimate. By Hölder’s inequality with (
3.15
) and (
3.24
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{m,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}|I_{m}|2^{2H(n){\delta}m}2^{\frac{3\min(\mathbf{k})-k_{2}}{2}}2^{(1+{\delta}_{1})k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(0)+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

(6.28)

This estimate implies (
6.27
) if
\min(\mathbf{k})\leq-m
.

Case 2:
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
and
2^{k}\leq 2^{-\frac{3}{5}m}
.
We claim that

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm Case\,2}|\mathcal{K}_{m,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.

(6.29)

When
2^{k}\leq 2^{-\frac{3}{5}m}
and
2^{k_{1}}\leq 2^{-\frac{2}{5}m}
, (
6.29
) follows from (
6.28
). When

2^{k}\leq 2^{-\frac{3}{5}m}\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}2^{(1+{\delta}_{1})k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-35}\leq 2^{-m},

(
6.29
) can be shown treated similarly. Then, it remains to prove (
6.29
) when

\displaystyle 2^{-m}\leq 2^{k}\leq 2^{-\frac{3}{5}m}\;\;\mbox{ and }\;\;2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}2^{(1+{\delta}_{1})k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-35}\geq 2^{-m}.

(6.30)

From this relation, we deduce
2^{k_{1}}\geq 2^{-\frac{2}{5}m}
. Setting
2^{J}:=C\left\langle s\right\rangle 2^{k_{1}}
for some constant
0<C\ll 1
, we decompose
{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}=\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}+\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}
as follows:
\mathcal{K}_{m,\mathbf{k}}
is the sum of the integrals:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}},}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,\end{aligned}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}},}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,\end{aligned}

(6.32)

where
p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)
is in (
2.4
). By (
3.26
) and (
3.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|e^{-{\theta_{1}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}2^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{1000})k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-19},

\displaystyle\|\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1+H(1){\delta}}2^{-k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)-1}.

This leads us to the bound

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:bulk-2-1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\sup_{s\in I_{m}}\|{P_{k}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{-{\theta_{1}}it{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{\leq J,k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m-\frac{m}{2}}2^{-\frac{k_{1}}{2}}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-19}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

When
\min(\mathbf{k})=k
, we have, for small
0<\zeta\ll 1
,

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:bulk-2-1}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}2^{-\frac{m}{2}+\zeta m}2^{\zeta k_{2}}2^{-k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-19}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)},

which implies the desired bound for (
6.31
) from the fact that
k_{1}\geq-\frac{2}{5}m
. When
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{2}
or
k_{1}
, by the second condition of (
6.30
), we directly obtain the desired results. Analogously, using Bernstein’s inequality, we estimate

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:bulk-2-2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}2^{k}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\sup_{s\in I_{m}}\|{P_{k}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}^{>J,k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}W_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{[H(1)+2H(n)]{\delta}m}2^{k-k_{1}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

Since
2^{k-k_{1}}\lesssim 2^{-\frac{1}{5}m}
, this finishes the proof for
Case 2
.

Case 3:
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
,
2^{k}\geq 2^{-\frac{3}{5}m}
, and
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\leq 2^{20{\delta}m}
.
Using the integration by parts in time,
\mathcal{K}_{m,\mathbf{k}}
is decomposed by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\frac{M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)}{p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}},}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds,\end{aligned}

(6.33)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{I_{m}}q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3+3}}\frac{M_{n}^{2}(\xi,\eta)}{p_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}e^{isp_{\Theta}(\xi,\eta)}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\partial_{s}\left\langle\widehat{P_{k}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}}(s,\xi),\widehat{P_{k_{2}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}},}}(s,\eta){\alpha}^{\mu}\widehat{P_{k_{1}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}}(s,\xi+\eta)\right\rangle d\xi d\eta ds.\end{aligned}

(6.34)

Using (
3.15
), (
4.2
), (
4.16
), and (
3.19
), we estimate

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|{P_{k}}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{m}\|{P_{k}}(\partial_{s}\phi_{{\theta_{2}},\mathcal{L}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{-N(n+1)-5},\\
\|e^{-{\theta_{1}}is{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{\frac{k_{1}}{2}}2^{-m+10{\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2},\\
\|{P_{k_{2}}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{m}\|{P_{k_{2}}}(\partial_{s}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta_{2}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}2^{\frac{k_{2}}{2}}2^{\widetilde{H}(n){\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)+5}.\end{aligned}

(6.35)

This yields that

\displaystyle\left|\eqref{eq:bulk3-1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{-m+(2\widetilde{H}(n)+10){\delta}m}2^{\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)-N(n+1)-5}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)+5}.

In (
6.34
), when the time derivative falls on
{P_{k}}\phi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}
or
{P_{k_{2}}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},{\theta_{2}}}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\left|\eqref{eq:bulk3-2}\right|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{-2m+(2\widetilde{H}(n)+10){\delta}m}2^{\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)-N(n+1)-5}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)+5}.

It remains to handle the case that
\partial_{s}
falls on
{P_{k_{1}}}\phi_{{\theta_{1}}}
in (
6.34
). By the condition of
Case 3
, (
3.20
) and (
3.23
) show us that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|e^{-{\theta_{1}}is{\left\langle D\right\rangle}}&{P_{k_{1}}}(\partial_{s}\phi_{\theta_{1}}(s))\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\sim\|\Pi_{{\theta_{1}}}(D){P_{k_{1}}}(A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(s))\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3\zeta k_{1}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}(A_{\mu}(s){\alpha}^{\mu}\psi(s))\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\zeta}}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3\zeta k_{1}}\sum_{\theta_{3},\theta_{4}\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}\in\mathcal{U}_{k_{\ell}}\\
k_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ell=3,4\end{subarray}}\|Q_{j_{3},k_{3}}\psi_{\theta_{3}}(s)\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\zeta}}}\|Q_{j_{4},k_{4}}A_{\mu,\theta_{4}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{2}2^{-2m+12{\delta}m}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}\end{aligned}

(6.36)

for sufficiently small
\zeta>0
. Note that we used the frequency relation
2^{k_{1}}\sim 2^{\max(k_{3},k_{4})}
in the fourth inequality. Then we have

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:bulk3-2}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{-2m+20{\delta}m}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}.

Since
\max(\mathbf{k})\leq 4{\delta}_{1}m
, we readily obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm Case\;3}|\mathcal{K}_{m,\,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.

Case 4:
2^{\min(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{-m}
,
2^{k}\geq 2^{-\frac{3}{5}m}
, and
2^{\max(\mathbf{k})}\geq 2^{20{\delta}m}
.
We prove

\displaystyle\sum_{\rm Case\;4}|\mathcal{K}_{m,\,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{2H(n){\delta}m}.

(6.37)

To this end, we consider the subcases
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{1}
,
\min(\mathbf{k})={k_{2}}
, and
\min(\mathbf{k})=k
.
If
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{1}
, then by the multiplier estimates, we see that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{m,\,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim|I_{m}|2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{2N(n)}\|{P_{k}}\psi_{\theta,\mathcal{L}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|{P_{k_{1}}}\psi_{{\theta_{1}}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{P_{k_{2}}}V_{\mu,\mathcal{L},\theta_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}.

Hence (
3.15
) and (
3.19
) show that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{m,\,\mathbf{k}}|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{1}^{3}2^{(10+2H(n)){\delta}m}2^{-\frac{k_{2}}{2}}2^{\frac{k_{1}}{2}}\left\langle 2^{k}\right\rangle^{N(n)}\left\langle 2^{k_{1}}\right\rangle^{-N(1)+2}\left\langle 2^{k_{2}}\right\rangle^{-N(n)}.

(6.38)

The RHS of (
6.38
) is summable over
Case 4
with
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{1}
.

For the high frequency sum with respect to
k
and
k_{2}
, (
6.38
) is not sufficient for (
6.37
) in the case of
\min(\mathbf{k})=k
or
\min(\mathbf{k})=k_{2}
. If
\min(\mathbf{k})=k
, then we use
